## 🆕 `p599` — `2026-11-01` — the market-size disagreement is in the GROWTH RATE, not in the 2026 base, and that reverses the deck rule this base has been carrying

### 🟢 Five firms measured this pass, and the `2026` bases agree

| firm | `2026` base | endpoint | CAGR | grade |
| --- | --: | --- | --: | --- |
| Trend X Insights | **`$7.30 B`** | `$61.26 B` by `2034` | `31.5 %` | secondary |
| Precedence Research | **`$9.58 B`** | `$136.79 B` by `2035` | `34.52 %` | secondary |
| Resourcera (citing) | **`$10.4 B`** | `$32.27 B` by `2030` | `31.2 %` | tertiary |
| Research and Markets | **`$10.6 B`** | `$42.48 B` by `2030` | `41.5 %` | secondary, report dated `2026-02` |
| Grand View Research | **`$11.4 B`** | `$57.2 B` by `2033` | `25.9 %` | secondary, already carried |

🟢 **Every one of the five falls inside the `$7 B–$11.4 B` band this page already publishes.** The
band is `5` passes old and it survived a fresh five-firm sweep without moving — which is a stronger
result than a revision would have been.

🔴 **The endpoints do not agree, and the spread is `4.3×`**: `$32.27 B` (Resourcera, `2030`) against
`$136.79 B` (Precedence, `2035`). 🔵 **So the disagreement between firms is a disagreement about the
growth rate, not about today's market.** Precedence states the mechanism itself — its figures
*"depend on market boundaries and the treatment of embedded AI revenues"*.

🔵 **This refines, and partly reverses, the rule this base adopted at `p93`.** That rule
(*"never put a market size in an education deck without naming the scope"*) was derived from the
MarketsandMarkets regional series summing to `≈ $2.07 B` against a `$7–11.4 B` band — a **scope**
failure. Measured here, scope is not the whole story: 🟢 **the `2026` base is robust across firms and
safe to quote; it is the forecast endpoint that is firm-specific and must never be quoted alone.**
New rule, additive rather than replacing: **quote the `2026` base (five firms agree inside `±22 %` of
the midpoint); if an endpoint is required, name the firm, the year and the CAGR in the same
sentence.** Registered as **`599-E`**.

### 🔴 A `$12.5 B` figure was served this pass and is NOT banked

A trends-leg source reports the market *"surged past the `$12.5 billion` milestone in `2026`"*, and a
second reports `$12.3 B`. 🔴 **Neither is corroborated by any of the five research firms above, and
both exceed the highest of them (`$11.4 B`).** Both are vendor/aggregator blogs. 🟢 **Rejected, not
averaged in** — and recorded here so a later pass does not re-find them and read two mutually
citing blogs as two sources. 🔵 **The band's ceiling does not move on blog evidence.**

### 🔴 The LATAM provenance trap this pass caught before filing

The LATAM leg served: *"la PUC firmó con Microsoft el programa **ConectIA**, que busca una
integración completa de la IA a nivel institucional para el año **2026**"* — a clean,
quotable, region-placed engagement reference. 🔴 **`ConectIA` was `0`-hits in this entire base**, so
it would have been filed as new regional intelligence.

🟢 **Checked against the primary institution first.** `uc.cl`'s own newsroom dates the UC
Chile–Microsoft ConectIA launch to **`2024`** (*"the first step … was taken at the end of last
year"*, under rector Ignacio Sánchez, who left office in March `2025`), and `2026` UC AI coverage —
the Chile–California AI mission — **does not mention ConectIA at all**. 🔴 **So the aggregator
re-dated a `2024` initiative as a `2026` target.** `ConectIA` stays unshelved as `2026` supply and is
filed here as a caution. 🔵 **The general shape, worth more than the instance: a secondary LATAM
aggregator will carry a real programme with a *forward* date attached, and the forward date is where
the fabrication lives — not in the programme name.** Registered as **`599-F`**.

### 🔴 Three primary legal hosts unreachable, so the Annex III date is STILL secondary-only

| host | result | consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | Commission's own AI Act timeline unread |
| `eur-lex.europa.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` primary text unread |
| `artificialintelligenceact.eu` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` (DNS) | the standard third-party tracker unread |

🟢 **Re-corroborated at secondary grade, and the dates this base carries did not move**: the Digital
Omnibus — **Regulation (EU) `2026/1744` of `2026-07-08`, in force `2026-07-27`** — moved stand-alone
Annex III high-risk obligations to **`2027-12-02`** and AI embedded in regulated products to
**`2028-08-02`**, while **Article 4 AI literacy has applied since `2025-02-02`** and is unaffected.
Source grade: a guide updated `2026-09-01`. Enforcement by the AI Office and national authorities
**began `2026-08-02`** — 🔵 **which is precisely the date that keeps being misread as the Annex III
deadline.**

🔴 **And the wrong reading was served for the SIXTH consecutive pass**, in the same sweep: *"several
guides say high-risk education obligations start in August 2026"*. 🔵 **Six instances in six passes
is no longer an error to correct; it is the market's majority reading** — so an EMEA education client
has probably been given `2026-08-02` by their installed advisor, and the first deliverable of an
EMEA engagement is the corrected clock, with the `2026-08-02` enforcement date explained as the
thing their advisor actually saw.

### 🔴 The regional legs: `4 of 4` paid, `0 of 4` served a token this base lacks

Eighth consecutive pass at `4 of 4` paid. Tokens served and already held: North America — `134`
bills / `31` states, the four states mandating both state guidance and district policy
(Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia), the `K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026`, the
April `2026` ADA Title II accessibility deadline, the `~70 %` Europe-and-North-America guidance
composite; EMEA — Reg. `2026/1744`, Article 4, the `Apply AI Alliance`, deployer classification;
APAC — Korea's `AI Basic Act` (in force `2026-01-22`, high-risk grace to `2027`), India's
compulsory AI from `Class 3` in `2026-27`, China's mandatory content labelling, Japan's promotion-only
`AI Promotion Act`; LATAM — UNESCO IESALC/UNU `200` institutions / `19` countries, Brazil's
`PL 2338/2023`, Chile's `2021` national AI policy, Mexico's absent federal AI law.
🔴 **Every one already carried.** The one *apparently* new token was `ConectIA`, refuted above.

🔵 **Saturation on the demand side is now the stable result, not the news** — and `599-B` explains
why it coexists with a live supply channel: this base's demand legs and its supply legs are
*differently* exhausted, and only the supply side responds to a change of ordering.

## 🆕 `p598` — `2026-11-01` — the legs pay `4 of 4` regionally for a seventh consecutive pass, and this pass states the evidence *grade* of every figure it publishes

🟢 **`4 of 4` regional legs paid.** Every region below carries at least one token this base has not
previously recorded, and all four are policy- or programme-shaped rather than vendor-shaped.

🔴 **`598-G` — and all of it is snippet-grade, which is now stated rather than implied.** The
primary sources these figures come from cannot be opened in this environment **on either
transport**:

| host | `curl` | fabricated control | `WebFetch` | discriminating |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `unu.edu` | `000` | 🔴 `000` | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | **NO** |
| `publications.iadb.org` | `000` | 🔴 `000` | — | **NO** |
| `oecd.org` | `000` | 🔴 `000` | — | **NO** |
| `unesco.org` | `000` | 🔴 `000` | — | **NO** |
| `newsletters.qs.com` | — | — | 🔴 `ENOTFOUND` | **NO** |

🔵 **Why this matters more than it looks.** `583-E` has recorded these hosts as `000` for `15`
passes, but a `000` with a `000` control certifies nothing — the host may be blocked, slow or
fictional and the probe cannot tell. `WebFetch` resolves it: `ENOTFOUND` means the host is outside
this session's allowed network domains. 🟢 So the channel is **closed, not flaky**, and no future
pass should spend legs trying to re-open it. 🔴 **Every regional figure on this page is therefore
search-snippet-grade with its source named, and cannot be raised to primary-source grade here.**
They are published with the grade attached rather than dropped, because an attributed number a
reader can discount is information and a silent omission looks exactly like coverage.

🔴 **The twenty-eighth consecutive nil on new addresses** — `8` legs, `8` repo-shaped addresses
served, `7` on a shelf page and `1` in the append-only log only, `0` new. Gated on the two channels
*separately*, per `597-D`. Detail on [`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md).

## 🆕 p597 — 2026-11-01 — the legs pay `4 of 4` for a sixth consecutive pass and the nil is now *proven* rather than assumed, because the gate that reported it was rebuilt first

### 🔵 The gate was wrong before the legs were run, so the legs were re-gated

`596-C` found that this base's "already held?" check greps the **append-only trending logs**, which
never forget, while the shelves are rewritten wholesale — so a dropped row reads as held. 🔴 **That
defect also contaminated this channel's nil**: a market token found only in a log was being counted
as coverage. 🟢 **The corrected gate reads the shelf and the logs as separate answers**, and it was
run against all `24` tokens the eight legs served before any of them was called new.

| token served | region | shelf | log | verdict |
| --- | --- | --: | --: | --- |
| **`134` bills across `31` states**; NA share **`36–38 %`**, **`$3.68bn`** 2026 | North America | `2` | `2` | 🔴 held — **eighth** consecutive pass |
| Gallup: **`>60 %`** of college students use AI weekly; ~half of institutions discourage or prohibit | North America | `4` | `1` | 🔴 held |
| **`$169m`** FIPSE awards with a responsible-AI component | North America | `3` | `2` | 🔴 held |
| 🆕 **District adoption of tools that *bypass the teacher* is in significant decline** | North America | 🟢 `0` | 🟢 `0` | 🟢 **NEW — the only new token of `24`** |
| Commission ethical guidelines on AI in education updated; **€25.4m** Advanced Digital Skills grants | EMEA | `2` | `1` | 🔴 held |
| UK **AI Adoption Summit**, **£200m+** (£100m Bridge AI, £53m regional) | EMEA | `2` | `1` | 🔴 held |
| EU AI Act **AI-literacy duties in force `2025-02-02`**; Annex III high-risk `2027-12-02` | EMEA | `2` | `2` | 🔴 held — `55`+ files |
| **`~70 %`** of institutions in Europe and North America have or are drafting AI guidance | EMEA | `4` | `2` | 🔴 held |
| **Project S.I.N.A.G.** — Philippines DepEd + Google, `>1m` teachers certified on Gemini | APAC | `2` | `0` | 🔴 held |
| Australia/NZ **National AI Plan** and **Chief AI Officers**; **ASEAN Guide** still early-stage | APAC | `1–2` | `0–1` | 🔴 held |
| Forrester: **`60 %`** of firms/governments in India and China to run local LLMs alongside global | APAC | `3` | `1` | 🔴 held |
| Digital Education Council × Tec de Monterrey: **`92 %`** students / **`79 %`** faculty using AI | LATAM | `4` | `2` | 🔴 held |
| Teachers' positive opinion **`57 % → 72 %`** in 2026; **`88 %`** use it only moderately | LATAM | `4` | `2` | 🔴 held |
| UNESCO **IESALC / UNU** working paper, `200` HEIs across `19` countries | LATAM | `7` | `2` | 🔴 held — `57` files |
| **IDB / CEPAL** — the Latin American AI Index (**ILIA**), `19` countries | LATAM | `3→0` | `1` | 🟡 **ILIA held `7×`; "Inter-American Development Bank" is 🔴 log-only — gate-blind** |
| Market frame: **`$8.3bn` (2025) → `$11.4bn` (2026) → `$57.2bn` (2033)**; K-12 **`~34 %`** share | Global | `3` | `2` | 🔴 held |

🟢 **`22 of 24` tokens are held on the shelf itself, `1` is new, `1` is log-only.** 🔴 **So the
market-intel nil is real, and this is its twenty-eighth consecutive pass** — it is a property of a
saturated demand-side channel, not of a broken gate. 🔵 **That is the opposite of what the same
correction did to the *address* channel, where `2,049` addresses turned out to be gate-blind and
`20` were promoted this pass.** `27` passes of undifferentiated "nil" could not tell these apart;
they are now separate measurements and must stay separate.

## Opportunities by region

### North America

🆕 **`p599` — the buyer's clock, not the buyer's budget, is what moved.** Two `2026` deadlines now
sit in the same fiscal year and both are *compliance* spend a studio can attach to: the **April `2026`
ADA Title II accessibility deadline** for large public institutions, and the four states that mandate
**both** state guidance and district-level AI policy (**Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma, Virginia**).
🔵 **Read together with the `134` bills / `31` states figure this page already carries, the North
American sale is a *policy-and-accessibility remediation* engagement that happens to need AI, not an
AI engagement that happens to need policy.** 🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged:** still no primary-source
instrument for **Canada or Mexico** — `583-E` holds, no government host resolves from this session.

🆕 **`p598` — the region's own figures disagree by `3.9×` and that spread is the finding.** The
legs served three 2026 sizings of the North American AI-in-education market this pass: **`$3.68 B`
at a `31.1 %` CAGR to `$32 B` by 2030** (marketsandmarkets), **`$951 M` in 2024 at `15.9 %` to
2029** (technavio), and a regional **share of `36 %`–`41.7 %`** depending on source. 🔴 **Do not
put any one of these in a client deck as the market size.** 🟢 **What is safely sayable**: North
America is the largest regional share on every source, and the dispersion between vendor sizings
is wider than the growth any of them forecasts.

🟢 **The governance figure is the usable one, because it is comparative.** `~70 %` of institutions
in Europe *and* North America have or are developing AI guidance, against `45 %` in Latin America
and the Caribbean. 🔵 A `25`-point governance gap is a procurement fact: a North American
engagement starts inside an existing policy and must comply with it; a LATAM engagement is often
asked to *write* it (see LATAM, below). 🟢 Public money has turned: the US Department of Education
released **`$169 M`** in FIPSE awards including responsible AI use in higher education.

🔴 **Regulation unchanged in shape and still the only quantified instrument here:** `134` bills
across `31` states, no comprehensive federal statute, **COPPA** governing K-12 product design, and
Canada as a second regime multinationals must reconcile. 🔵 The OECD's *Digital Education Outlook
2026* is cited as arguing AI helps learning **only where pedagogy stays in control** — which is the
demand-side reading behind this pass's
[`nietzschian-debugger`](https://github.com/DilawarShafiq/nietzschian-debugger) promotion.

🟢 **Supply added this pass for this region.** [`quickfeed/quickfeed`](https://github.com/quickfeed/quickfeed)
(MIT, byte-read `1,115 B`) and [`gradescope/gradescope-utils`](https://github.com/gradescope/gradescope-utils)
(MIT, `1,070 B`) give the auto-graded-coursework seam a **permissive** stack where the base
previously had `infomark-org/infomark` (🔴 GPL-3.0). 🔵 Both are under `40★` — institution-grade
code a star-ranked screen cannot see.

🆕 **`p597` — the one new demand-side token in `24` legs is a *shape* change, and it is commercially
load-bearing.** District adoption of tools that **bypass the teacher** is in significant decline.
🔵 Read against this base's standing North American reading — governance-plus-integration against
Canvas and PowerSchool estates — it narrows the proposal: **the buyer is the teacher's workflow, not
the student's**, and a studio pitching a direct-to-student tutor into a US district in 2026 is
pitching into a declining category.

🟢 **Supply recovered this pass, and it is the roster seam.**
[`ed-fi-alliance-oss/edfi-oneroster`](https://github.com/ed-fi-alliance-oss/edfi-oneroster)
(Apache-2.0, byte-read `10,173 B`, pushed `2026-10-31`) serves a **OneRoster 1.2 API from an Ed-Fi
ODS** — the US K-12 data standard — at `6★`. 🔵 **A star-ranked screen cannot see institution-grade
interop code**, which is the same mechanism `p596` recorded for FOLIO. Paired with
[`r-huijts/canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/r-huijts/canvas-mcp) (MIT, `1,065 B`) the region now has
both halves of an agent attach point: roster data and LMS actions. 🔴 Unchanged and still the only
quantified instrument this channel produces: `134` bills across `31` states.

### EMEA

🆕 **`p599` — the clock was re-corroborated and the market's wrong reading is now at six instances in
six passes.** Annex III stand-alone obligations: **`2027-12-02`**; embedded in regulated products:
**`2028-08-02`**; Article 4 AI literacy: **live since `2025-02-02`**; AI Office and national-authority
**enforcement began `2026-08-02`**. 🔴 **`2026-08-02` is the date the market keeps quoting as the
Annex III deadline**, and it was served that way again this pass. 🔵 **The engagement consequence:
the first EMEA deliverable is the corrected timeline plus an Article 4 literacy programme that is
already overdue — the only obligation here that is in force today.** 🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged:**
no EMEA-only education adoption rate exists; the `~70 %` figure is a Europe-**and**-North-America
composite and must not be presented as an EMEA number.

🆕 **`p598` — EMEA is the one region where the binding instrument is a *statute*, not a budget.**
The EU AI Act's **AI-literacy obligation has been in force since `2025-02-02`** and it reaches
schools, universities and training providers directly — not as guidance but as a duty. 🟢 The
Commission added the operational layer this year: **updated ethical guidelines on AI in education**
(`9 June`) and **three Advanced Digital Skills grant agreements worth `€25.4 M`**. 🔵 For a studio
this inverts the usual sales motion: in EMEA the client has a **compliance deadline**, so an
AI-literacy and governance workstream is the entry point and the tutor is the follow-on.

🟢 **The UK is funding adoption rather than regulating it:** `£200 M+` committed at its first **AI
Adoption Summit** (`8 June`). 🔴 The sources place that money in workplaces, **not schools** —
recorded as adjacent, not as an education budget.

🔵 **Readiness, enterprise-wide rather than education-specific:** Workday puts `56 %` of EMEA CEOs
as prepared for AI adoption against `48 %` in the Americas and `45 %` in APJ — EMEA leads on
self-reported readiness. CompTIA has `94 %` of organisations at least somewhat likely to invest in
AI-specific training in 2026. 🔴 Both are enterprise samples; neither is a school or university
adoption rate, and this pass could not find one.

🟢 **Supply note.** The Apereo consortium (`598-D`) is disproportionately **European university**
infrastructure — Sakai, uPortal and CAS run in EMEA estates — and a licence screen reading the
GitHub API's `spdx_id` rejects most of it as unlicensed. 🔵 Fixing that screen is worth more to an
EMEA engagement than any new repository found this pass.

🔵 **The `p593` reading stands unamended: regulation ahead of capability, so the deliverable is
capability.** 🆕 **What `p597` adds is that the UK's open-curriculum tier is already
agent-addressable.** [`oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem`](https://github.com/oaknational/oak-open-curriculum-ecosystem)
(MIT — grant file spelled **`LICENCE`**, byte-read `1,086 B`, pushed `2026-11-01`) ships **API, SDK
*and* an MCP app** over Oak National Academy's curriculum data. 🟢 **So an EMEA engagement does not
have to build the curriculum-grounding layer; it can consume one that a national body maintains**,
which is exactly the "capability, not compliance theatre" deliverable this region's opening calls
for. 🔴 Note the spelling: a licence screen probing `LICENSE` only would have blocked this row.

🟡 **Demand-side, carried:** €25.4m Advanced Digital Skills grants and the UK's £200m+ adoption
commitment are **cross-sector** envelopes, and this page continues to refuse to read them as
education budgets.

### APAC

🆕 **`p599` — the region's regulatory asymmetry is now datable, and one national rollout already
failed in public.** Korea's **`AI Basic Act` took effect `2026-01-22`** — the region's first
comprehensive AI statute — with a **grace period for legacy high-risk systems in health, education
and finance running to `2027`**; India makes **AI compulsory from `Class 3` in the `2026-27` academic
year** under the IndiaAI Mission with no standalone AI statute yet; China runs mandatory generative-AI
filing and **content labelling since `2025-09`**; Japan's `AI Promotion Act` carries no penalties.
🔴 **The cautionary datum, and it is the most useful thing in this subsection:** Korea's mandatory
**AI textbooks reached under `30 %` adoption** and the National Assembly **removed their official
status** after teacher unions said the rollout had outrun teacher preparation. 🔵 **A national mandate
is not a national deployment — in APAC the binding constraint is teacher readiness, which is the
service line, and Korea is the reference failure to cite when a ministry proposes a content mandate.**
🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged:** no APAC ministry-level adoption *rate* — named programmes only, never
a denominator.

🆕 **`p598` — APAC's 2026 organising idea is sovereignty, and it has a concrete education shape.**
Forrester expects **`60 %` of firms and governments in major markets such as India and China to run
locally developed LLMs alongside global ones**, naming **education** among the target sectors, and
regional commentary frames 2026 as the year *"AI sovereignty sets the pace"*. 🔵 Read as a
procurement constraint this is the strongest argument in any region for the **self-hosted,
permissively licensed** stack this KB curates: a sovereignty requirement rules out a hosted
US tutor and rules **in** an on-premise assembly.

🟢 **The largest concrete education programme served by any leg this pass is APAC's.** The
Philippines' **Department of Education with Google — Project S.I.N.A.G. — is certifying more than
`1 million` teachers** in Gemini. 🔵 Note what it is: a **teacher-capability** programme at national
scale, not a student tutor. 🔴 It is also a closed-stack national commitment, which is the
competitive fact a studio should plan around rather than bid against.

🔴 **Regulation is early-stage and per-country.** The expectation on offer is harmonisation on
**principles** — safety, transparency, accountability — *executed differently in each market*, so
compliance cost scales with the number of jurisdictions rather than with the size of the
deployment. 🔵 This is why `P-597-C`'s policy-node pattern is an APAC pattern.

🟢 **In-language supply added this pass.** [`ERRRC/kaogong-shuati`](https://github.com/ERRRC/kaogong-shuati)
(MIT, byte-read `1,062 B`, `151★`) — Chinese civil-service exam practice with an error notebook and
AI explanations — joins [`SimonsTang/feifei-companion`](https://github.com/SimonsTang/feifei-companion)
(Apache-2.0) and [`KeWang0622/kaogong-skill`](https://github.com/KeWang0622/kaogong-skill) (MIT).
🔵 **APAC is the only region whose in-language education supply is reliably permissive** — the
contrast with LATAM below is now four passes old and stable.

🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged:** the legs again returned **no APAC school or university adoption
rate**. The APAC numbers available are enterprise surveys (Omdia/Boomi, `1,100+` decision-makers
across Australia, New Zealand, Singapore, Malaysia and the Philippines), not education ones.

🔴 **Nothing new for this region this pass, stated explicitly rather than left as silence.** The
legs returned Project S.I.N.A.G., the ASEAN Guide, Australia/NZ's National AI Plan and Forrester's
local-LLM figure — all four already held on the shelf.

🟢 **Supply side did move.** [`sunbird-ed/sunbirded-portal`](https://github.com/sunbird-ed/sunbirded-portal)
(MIT, `1,072 B`) is recovered to [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) — India's
DPG platform tier, the only **national-scale** permissive platform of record on these shelves. 🔴 It
has not been pushed since `2026-02-21` and is `334 MB`, so it is a reference architecture to mine,
not a dependency to track. 🔵 In-language supply grows again with
[`simonstang/feifei-companion`](https://github.com/simonstang/feifei-companion) (Apache-2.0,
`10,227 B`, K-12, Chinese), joining DeepTutor, OpenMAIC and pupilfirst.

### LATAM

🆕 **`p599` — this region produced this pass's one disconfirmed finding, and the disconfirmation is
the deliverable.** The leg served `ConectIA` (UC Chile + Microsoft) as a `2026` institutional-AI
programme; UC Chile's own newsroom dates it to **`2024`** and `2026` UC AI coverage never mentions it
(`599-F`, full working above). 🔴 **Not filed.** 🔵 **So the standing LATAM rule gets sharper: the
aggregators in this region report real institutions with invented forward dates — verify the *date*
against the institution's own site, not the programme name.** 🟢 The region's best evidence is still
the **UNESCO IESALC / UNU survey — `200` institutions, `19` countries** — carried at snippet grade
because `unu.edu` and `unesco.org` do not resolve here. 🔴 **Declared gap, unchanged:** no LATAM
education-specific capital figure beyond one forecast.

🆕 **`p598` — LATAM now has a *primary* evidence base for higher education, and this environment
cannot open it.** A UNU working paper reports a survey of **`200` institutions across `19`
countries**, run **August–October 2025**, mapping AI adoption across teaching, research,
administration and governance in Latin America and the Caribbean — the single most relevant
document served by any leg this pass. 🔴 `unu.edu` returns `000` on `curl` and `ENOTFOUND` on
`WebFetch` (`598-G`): **it is cited here at snippet grade and flagged as unread**, not paraphrased
as if measured. 🟢 Same for the IDB's **fAIr Tech Radar** (November 2025).

🔴 **The region's defining number is a deficit, and it is comparative.** `45 %` of LAC institutions
have or are developing AI guidance against `~70 %` in Europe and North America — a **`25`-point
governance gap**. 🟢 **This is the LATAM engagement thesis and it should be sold as such**: the
first deliverable is frequently the *policy and governance scaffold*, which North American and EMEA
clients already own. Baker McKenzie's reading supports it — **no Latin American country has a fully
mature, harmonised AI regime**, and regional policy is a patchwork of pilots *"vulnerable to being
tossed out with each new administration"*. 🔵 Policy instability is itself a design requirement:
build the governance layer as configuration, not as code.

🔴 **Two constraints that bound any LATAM proposal.** Infrastructure — reliable internet and devices
remain uneven, so **offline-first is a requirement, not a feature** (which is why
[`learningequality/kolibri`](https://github.com/learningequality/kolibri) anchors `P-597-B`). And
UNESCO's flagged **severe teacher shortage**, which argues for teacher-multiplier tooling over
student-facing tutors. 🟢 Investment is rising: `~$2.5 B` of AI investment expected in the region in
2026.

🟢 **Supply, and the four-pass-old asymmetry is unchanged.** [`guillermoscript/lms-front`](https://github.com/guillermoscript/lms-front)
(MIT, byte-read `1,072 B`, pushed `2026-11-01`) is promoted this pass and is the **first row on
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) that is multi-tenant, MCP-ready and ships
an `es` locale** — a genuine LATAM-ready starting point. 🔴 Against that: the Spanish- and
Portuguese-language *tutors* this base has measured remain **unlicensed almost without exception**
(`591-C`), and this pass adds the exam-prep counterpart —
[`rodrigoborgesmachado/questoesConcursos`](https://github.com/rodrigoborgesmachado/questoesConcursos)
(`31★`, Brazilian public-exam practice, 🔴 no grant) against APAC's MIT-licensed
[`kaogong-shuati`](https://github.com/ERRRC/kaogong-shuati). 🔵 **Identical demand, opposite
licensing culture** — so in LATAM the studio builds; it does not adopt.

🟢 **Named players served this pass:** Ednova (Chile), Platzi, and Universidad Autónoma de
Guadalajara, which has published `15` AI-use guidelines for its students.

🔵 **The demand-side reading is unchanged and was re-gated, not re-argued:** `92 %` of students and
`79 %` of faculty already using AI against almost no institutional governance, now with the
direction that **teachers' positive opinion moved `57 % → 72 %` during 2026** while `88 %` still use
it only moderately. 🟢 The deliverable remains **governance for usage that already exists** — the
mirror image of EMEA — and the moving teacher sentiment makes enablement sellable alongside it.

🔴 **The supply-side finding of this pass is a closed door, and it is worth more than another
adoption statistic.** [`menthorlabs/menthor`](https://github.com/menthorlabs/menthor) (Apache-2.0,
`582★`) — the largest LATAM-origin **permissive** education platform this base has ever found — is
🔴 **ARCHIVED, last pushed `2024-04-21`**. With `594-D`'s finding that the live Brazilian school
systems (`portabilis/i-educar`, `i-diario`, `pre-matricula-digital`) are all copyleft, 🔴 **the
region's permissive platform-of-record tier is empty by measurement, not by omission.** 🔵 The
consequence for a LATAM engagement is concrete: **attach over APIs, do not fork**, and budget the
platform layer as integration rather than customisation.

### Global

🆕 **`p599` — the `2026` market base is now the only figure on this page that five independent firms
agree on, and the forecast endpoints disagree by `4.3×`.** Quote the base (`$7 B–$11.4 B`); never
quote an endpoint without naming the firm, the year and the CAGR beside it (`599-E`). 🔴 A `$12.5 B`
`2026` figure circulating in vendor blogs exceeds every research firm measured and is **rejected**.

🆕 **`p598` — the global market sizings span `3.1×` for the same year, so this base will not quote
one.** 2026 estimates served by the legs: **`$3.7 B`** (Persistence), **`$6.4 B`** for 2025
(IMARC), **`$11.4 B`** rising to `$57.2 B` by 2033. 🔴 A `$3.7 B`-to-`$11.4 B` spread for a single
year is not a forecast disagreement, it is a definitional one — the sources are not measuring the
same market. 🟢 **Use the structural facts instead**, which agree across sources: **K-12 is the
largest segment at `~34 %` of 2026 share**, and the growth is in adaptive learning built **into**
the LMS rather than sold alongside it.

🔴 **The structural change worth more than any sizing: the unit of sale is becoming the country.**
OpenAI launched a country-level education programme with **eight national partners** in Q1 2026, and
the Philippines–Google teacher-certification programme (APAC, above) is the same shape. 🔵 If
national infrastructure deals displace school-by-school and district-by-district sales, a studio's
addressable work shifts from *building the product* to **integrating and governing a national stack
it did not choose** — which favours interop, policy and migration capability over tutor-building.

🟢 **And the demand-side shift this base has now recorded for three passes:** the question has moved
from policing academic integrity to whether AI can be designed **into** pedagogy. 🔵 That is the
commercial reason
[`DilawarShafiq/nietzschian-debugger`](https://github.com/DilawarShafiq/nietzschian-debugger) —
an agent whose entire product is *refusing to give the answer* — is on
[`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md) this pass rather than in a curiosity list.

🔵 **Market frame, re-gated and unchanged:** `$8.3bn` (2025) → `$11.4bn` (2026) → `$57.2bn` (2033)
on one reading, with K-12 at **`~34 %`** of 2026 spend; this base continues to carry the range rather
than a point estimate, because the published figures disagree by more than `3×` at the 2026 node.
🟡 Every one of these is a market-research marketing page; none is audited.

🟢 **The global supply finding is this pass's own:** a **fully permissive education stack** can now be
assembled from rows on these shelves — Aureus ERP (back office), Agora Flat (synchronous classroom),
Autolab/Anubis (course delivery + autograding), JupyterHub (compute), Kolibri Studio (offline
packaging), FSRS-rs (retention), Learner Credential Wallet (credentialing). 🔵 **That was true before
this pass and unstatable, because `78.4 %` of what this base had found was dropped.**

### 🔴 Declared regional gaps — a nil in prose reads like coverage

| gap | status this pass |
| --- | --- |
| 🔴 No primary-source regulatory instrument for **Canada or Mexico** | Unclosed. `583-E` stands: no government or IGO host is reachable from this session |
| 🔴 No **APAC** ministry-of-education adoption *rate* | Unclosed — named programmes only, never denominators |
| 🔴 No **EMEA** education-sector AI adoption rate | 🟡 Partially addressed and **not** closed: `~70 %` of institutions "in Europe and North America" have or are drafting guidance is a **two-region composite**, not an EMEA rate |
| 🔴 No **LATAM** education-specific capital figure | Unclosed; `$4.2bn by 2030 (~45 % CAGR)` remains the only education-specific number and it is a forecast |
| 🆕 🔴 **No permissive LATAM platform of record exists** | 🟢 **Newly closed as a gap, negatively**: measured this pass, the one candidate is archived. This is now a finding, not a hole |

🔴 **`0 of 17` new. Twenty-seventh consecutive nil.** 🟢 **Stated as the prompt requires: no

### 🟢 This pass tested the nil instead of accepting it, and the test refuted the easy explanation

`p595` concluded the nil "is now a measured property of the channel." 🔵 Before accepting
that, this pass pre-registered the competing hypothesis — that this base's own "already
held?" gate is a **substring** `grep` and manufactures false "held" verdicts — in
[`../evidence/prereg-596A-held-gate-substring.2026-11-01-p596.md`](../evidence/prereg-596A-held-gate-substring.2026-11-01-p596.md),
**before measuring**.

🔴 **Refuted for the regional legs, and the reason is structural:** every token in the table
above is a long multi-word phrase (`Digital Omnibus`, `K-12 AI Literacy`, `Annex III`,
`KHDA`), and phrases of that length cannot collide by substring. Each was additionally
re-verified on the curated pages one at a time. 🟢 **The demand-side nil is real.**

🔵 **What the test did find is a gate hazard worth carrying:** substring and word-boundary
`grep` fail in *opposite* directions. Substring over-reports `held` (`AGS` `131` → `46`);
word-boundary collapses to **zero** on stems (`dyslex` `19` → `0`, `plagiari` `51` → `0`,
`timetabl` `62` → `2`), so a word-boundary "fix" would make this base re-file material it has
held all along. 🔴 **Neither is a valid gate on its own**; full table in
[`../evidence/gate-substring-vs-word.2026-11-01-p596.tsv`](../evidence/gate-substring-vs-word.2026-11-01-p596.tsv).

### 🔴 `596-C` — where this pass's value actually came from, and it is not the demand side

🔴 **The shelf is lossy, the log is lossless, and the gate that decides what gets shelved
consults the log.** `archive/` holds **`15`** snapshots named `pre-reset`, `pre-rewrite`,
`pre-trim`, `pre-consolidation`: the shelf pages are rewritten wholesale and lose rows each
time, while the two append-only trending pages lose nothing.

| population | count |
| --- | --: |
| ledger addresses | `3,174` |
| 🔵 logged on the trending pages (lossless) | `3,143` |
| 🟢 on a **current** shelf | **`234`** |
| 🔵 on **any** shelf ever (incl. `15` archived snapshots) | `2,243` |
| 🔴 **dropped** — shelved once, absent from every current shelf | 🔴 **`2,015`** (`89.8 %`) |
| never shelved anywhere | `921` |

🔴 **That is the mechanism of the nil.** `569-B` requires a token to be grepped against this
base before being called new, and that grep hits the logs — so a row dropped from a shelf
answers *"already held"* forever and is never re-shelved. 🟢 **`6` addresses were recovered
from that population this pass**, every grant byte-read, placed by region below. 🔵 **And it
reframes `595-A`:** the `118 MB` is not the problem — the `118 MB` is the only part of this
base that never loses anything. The problem is the rewrite.

### 🟢 `595-B` CLOSED

This file carried **three** `## Opportunities by region` headings at lines `105`, `204` and
`968`, so a compiler keying opportunities off that heading bucketed them three ways and read
order decided the winner. 🟢 **The file's own convention for a superseded copy already
existed** at four other places — `### (historical) … superseded, retained for history`. It is
now applied to both duplicates. 🟢 **Verified: `grep -c "^## Opportunities by region"` → `1`.**
No prose was deleted.

🔴 **`15 of 16` already held. The demand-side saturation `p591` and `p592` measured is holding at
the same rate for a fourth pass**, and it is now a stable property of this channel rather than a
run of bad luck: the mandated regional legs reliably recover this base's existing picture and
reliably do not extend it.

### 🟡 The one new token, with its provenance stated rather than assumed

🟢 **New:** total AI startup investment in Latin America is projected at **$2.5bn by end-2026**
(Emerging Markets Insights, report dated 3 Oct 2026, reached via secondary coverage), with Sequoia
Capital and Lightspeed named as increasing regional AI activity.

🔴 **Three caveats, all load-bearing, and the first is the one that matters for this KB:**

1. 🔴 **It is cross-sector AI capital, not education capital.** It does not measure edtech and must
   not be cited as an education figure. This base's education-specific LATAM number remains
   **$4.2bn by 2030 at ~45 % CAGR** (carried, `p565`).
2. 🔴 **Two outlets disagree on scope.** A competing tally puts LATAM AI startups at **$2bn raised
   year-to-date in 2026** — a count, not a full-year projection. The two are not comparable and
   neither was verified at source.
3. 🟡 **The structural direction is corroborated independently**, which is worth more than the
   point estimate: Crunchbase reports **LATAM startup funding up 26 % in Q3 2026** with **$757m**
   into late-stage and growth deals (**+74 % year on year**), and Endeavor reports international
   VCs more active in the region across the first three quarters of 2026 than in 2024 and 2025
   combined.

🔵 **So the usable finding is a direction, not a figure:** LATAM capital availability is rising
sharply from a low base, which changes what a studio can propose there — but it says nothing
specific about education budgets, and this page should not let it.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

### 🟢 North America

🆕 **`p596` — supply, recovered rather than discovered.** [`edanalytics/earthmover_edfi_bundles`](https://github.com/edanalytics/earthmover_edfi_bundles) (Apache-2.0, `14★`, pushed `2026-10-30`) ships pre-built mappings from assessment-vendor formats into the **Ed-Fi** API with automatic student-ID matching. 🔵 **It exists because most assessment vendors ship no native Ed-Fi integration** — which is the unglamorous integration work a US K-12 district engagement is actually billed for, and it is now on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md). 🟢 **Also new to this region's shelf: FOLIO**, a `456`-repository Apache-2.0 Library Services Platform with `0` copyleft repos — a separate university buyer and budget this page had never covered. See [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md).

🔵 **Unchanged this pass; restated so a nil does not read as coverage.** Largest regional share of
AI-in-education spend (**~36–38 %**, `$3.68bn` in 2026 on the `36 %` reading), the densest
regulatory activity at **state** level (`134` bills across `31` states — served for a fifth pass
and still the only quantified instrument this channel produces), and **$169m** in FIPSE awards
with a responsible-AI component. 🟢 **The engagement shape stays governance-plus-integration**
against Canvas and PowerSchool estates.

🆕 **What `p594` adds here is supply-side, not demand-side.**
[`PrairieLearn/PrairieLearn`](https://github.com/PrairieLearn/PrairieLearn) (`527★`, North America,
pushed `2026-11-01`) is confirmed again as a **split grant** — AGPL-3.0 community edition plus a
proprietary `ee/` tree — and this pass's classifier now escalates that shape to review instead of
resolving it, which is the correct behaviour for a row a studio must not fork casually.

### 🟢 EMEA

🆕 **`p596` — supply, recovered rather than discovered.** [`openpatch/hyperbook`](https://github.com/openpatch/hyperbook) (MIT, `75★`, Germany, pushed `2026-11-01`) builds interactive workbooks as static sites from a markdown engine with `30+` directives — needing no LMS and no server to deliver. 🔵 **It lands precisely on this region's stated opening**, which `p593` established is *capability* rather than compliance: a client that cannot operate what is shipped is the failure mode, and courseware that is a static site is the lowest-operating-burden artefact this page can offer. 🟢 FOLIO (above) is strong here too, on institutional library adoption.

🔵 **The `p593` reading stands and is not re-argued:** regulation ahead of capability, so the
deliverable is **capability** and the failure mode is a compliant system nobody can operate. The
AI literacy framework under the European Education Area 2026–2030 remains the commercial opening,
and enablement should be scoped and budgeted, not offered as courtesy.

🆕 **`p594`'s contribution is a permissive EMEA row recovered from a blank licence field.**
[`thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem`](https://github.com/thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem) (Technische Hochschule
Mittelhessen, Germany) reported `NOASSERTION` and is **Apache-2.0** — automated programming-exercise
feedback and grading, directly on the capability-building seam this region's opening calls for.
🔵 Small (`24★`), so it is a component to adopt, not a platform to stand on. 🟡 Also EMEA and
already shelved: `apache/openmeetings` (Apache-2.0 + a declared third-party block),
`claroline/Claroline` (AGPL-3.0, Belgian), `ILIAS` (GPL-3.0, German), `OpenOLAT` (Apache-2.0,
Swiss) — and `19` of the `30` branch-defect rows on
[`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md) are European platforms, which is where the
`594-B` census bites hardest in practice.

### 🟢 APAC

🔴 **`p596` — nothing new for this region, stated explicitly.** The legs returned Korea, Vietnam, TEQSA, Singapore, China and Japan, all held; and **none of the `6` addresses promoted this pass is APAC-origin**. 🔵 This region's supply position is unchanged and remains the strongest on these shelves (`DeepTutor`, `OpenMAIC`) — 🆕 **and `OpenMAIC` finally has a measured size: `42,265★` as of `2026-11-01`**, a figure this base held in `0` files before this pass despite holding the address in `243`. 🔴 The standing APAC gap — no ministry-level adoption *rate*, only named programmes — is **unclosed**.

🔵 **Restated, carried.** Policy is moving nationally rather than regionally: Australia's **National
AI Plan** and Chief AI Officers; the **ASEAN Guide on AI Governance and Ethics** still early-stage
and per-country; Singapore regulating sector by sector; the Philippines' **Project S.I.N.A.G.**
certifying ~1m teachers on Gemini — the one *named* national education programme this channel has
produced. Forrester's expectation that **60 %** of firms and governments in India and China will run
locally developed LLMs alongside global ones remains the sovereignty driver.

🔵 **Supply side, this pass:** APAC continues to hold the largest in-language education supply on
these shelves — `DeepTutor` (HKUDS), `OpenMAIC` (Tsinghua), `word-atlas`, `Study-Mate`,
`pupilfirst` (India, MIT with a `docs/` CC-BY-SA carve-out this pass refined). 🔴 **The one address
the global legs surfaced is APAC and unusable:**
[`anandpatikat/guru-latihan-dinamis`](https://github.com/anandpatikat/guru-latihan-dinamis)
(`115★`, Indonesian exam practice) has **no licence file at `13` paths** — `BLOCK`.

### 🟢 LATAM

🆕 **`p596` — an offline delivery route, which is this region's binding constraint rather than its licence position.** [`missing-elements/h5p-offline-player`](https://github.com/missing-elements/h5p-offline-player) (MIT, stable since `2026-10-18`) plays `.h5p` packages **with no H5P server, no Moodle and no backend**, and ships a PWA that runs with no network at all. 🔵 **Interactive content delivered to a school with intermittent connectivity, off static hosting**, is a different engagement from one that assumes an LMS is reachable. 🔴 **Licence caveat that must be priced:** the player is MIT, **the H5P runtime it loads is GPL-3.0 and published separately** — ship them as one artefact and you have taken a copyleft obligation. 🔴 The LATAM education-specific *capital* gap stays unclosed, and this pass added nothing to it.

🟡 **New capital signal, bounded:** `$2.5bn` cross-sector AI investment projected for 2026 (single
report, contested at `$2bn` YTD by another outlet), corroborated in *direction* by Crunchbase's
`+26 %` Q3 and Endeavor's three-quarter comparison. 🔴 **Not an education figure.** The
education-specific picture is unchanged: **$4.2bn by 2030 (~45 % CAGR)**, with `92 %` of students
and `79 %` of faculty already using AI against almost no institutional governance — so the
deliverable here remains **governance for usage that already exists**, the mirror image of EMEA.
🔵 Colombia's **Ley 2626 de 2026** remains the region's first binding education-AI instrument on
these shelves (`p576`).

🆕 **`p594` adds the supply-side half, and it is a correction to this base's own instrument.**
`591-C` found LATAM's in-language tutors unlicensed while its courseware was permissive. This pass
found that two live **Brazilian** school systems were being mis-read for a different reason
entirely: [`portabilis/i-diario`](https://github.com/portabilis/i-diario) and
[`portabilis/pre-matricula-digital`](https://github.com/portabilis/pre-matricula-digital) carry
**AGPL-3.0 written in Portuguese** — `LICENÇA PÚBLICA GERAL AFFERO GNU` — on branches `1.6` and
`2.12`. 🔴 **An English-only licence matcher on a `main`-only probe false-blocks both**, and
`portabilis/i-educar` (`723★`, `GPL-2.0`, branch `2.12`) with them. 🔵 **These are the largest
LATAM-origin education platforms on these shelves and all three are copyleft** — attach an AI
side-car over their APIs, do not fork. See `594-D`.

## 🆕 p593 — 2026-11-01 — the regional legs pay `4 of 4` again and serve `2` tokens this base has never held; both are EMEA, and together they say the regulation is ahead of the capability

### 🔵 The legs, measured against this base rather than described

| leg | tokens served | already held | 🟢 new |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| North America | `5` | `5` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| EMEA | `6` | `4` | 🟢 **`2`** |
| APAC | `5` | `5` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| LATAM | `6` | `6` | 🔴 **`0`** |

🟢 **`4 of 4` legs paid.** 🟡 `2 of 22` tokens new — the lowest demand-side yield yet recorded, and
consistent with `p591`–`p592`'s saturation finding rather than contradicting it.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

### 🟢 North America

- 36 % of the global AI-education market, ≈**$3.68 B in 2026** (MarketsandMarkets); a second
  house gives 38 % share for 2025. 🔵 Both already held — restated for provenance, not as new.
- 🔴 **No federal statute**; a patchwork of state law governs. In K-12 the purchasing driver is
  **child privacy (COPPA)**, where compliance has become a *sales argument* rather than a cost.
- 🟡 ≈$169 M US federal commitment to "responsible AI" in higher education, Q1 2026 — **still
  without a primary source** after four passes. Retained as a signal, never as a figure.
- 🟢 **Engagement shape:** migration off Canvas/Blackboard onto Open edX plus an agentic layer,
  with the COPPA/FERPA compliance dossier as a first-class deliverable. With no single federal
  rule, that dossier is work only an integrator can absorb.

### 🟢 EMEA — both new tokens landed here, and they point opposite ways

- 🟢 **NEW `593-F`:** the European Commission maintains an **AI literacy framework for primary and
  secondary education**, before the Council under the **European Education Area 2026–2030**
  cooperation agenda, titled **"Empowering learners for the age of AI"**. 🔵 Significance: the
  entry point in EMEA is **curriculum**, not platform — a literacy programme is the doorway to
  the stack modernisation behind it. The Council of Europe's AI & Education expert group
  separately reports a **lack of rigorous monitoring and regulation** and asks for coordinated
  national and European action.
- 🔴 **NEW `593-G`:** **Allianz Pulse 2026** finds Europe adopting AI **less extensively and far
  less work-adjacently than the US**, with wide internal spread and an explicit "preparedness
  gap".
- 🔵 **Read the two together:** EMEA has the most developed *regulation* and a lagging *capability*.
  🟢 So the risk on an EMEA engagement is not compliance — it is that the client cannot operate
  what is delivered. **Budget training, not just software.** This is a shift in emphasis from the
  compliance-first framing this page has carried since `p586`.
- UK, Germany and France hold **>60 %** of regional value; higher education is 45.7 % of share
  (2025). Already held.

### 🟢 APAC

- MarketsandMarkets: **$591.6 M (2024) → $1,848.1 M (2029), CAGR 20.9 %**, with the region
  described as fastest-growing. 🔴 **Internal contradiction, declared:** a 20.9 % CAGR is *half*
  the global 40.9 % while the same narrative calls it fastest-growing. The APAC series runs off a
  2024 baseline and is **not comparable** to the global figure. Never cite the two side by side.
- 🔴 **Regulatory fragmentation is structural, not transitional** — Forrester holds a common APAC
  framework unlikely, unlike the EU. 2026 regional priority is **sovereignty**. No
  education-*specific* AI regulation surfaced on this leg.
- 🟢 **Engagement shape:** per-jurisdiction policy nodes from day one; the fragmentation is the
  functional requirement, not the obstacle.
- 🔴 **Gap held open:** no credible 2026 ranking of APAC education-AI players (the only list found
  dates from 2019), and no country-level classroom data.

### 🟢 LATAM

- **92 % of students and 79 % of faculty actively using AI** (Digital Education Council ×
  Tecnológico de Monterrey, *AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026*). Already held since
  `p58x`; reconfirmed on this leg.
- 🔴 **The gap is governance, not adoption:** UNESCO IESALC (200 institutions, 19 countries)
  reports a pronounced split between adoption and institutional governance, with formal AI-use
  policy largely absent, and adoption "neither uniform nor evenly distributed" between public and
  private universities.
- Policy actors: UNESCO IESALC, UNU-IAS, Digital Education Council, Tec de Monterrey, IDB (**ILIA**
  index, 19 countries), ECLAC. Brookings frames the region as a natural **rights-based sandbox**.
- 🟢 **Engagement shape:** with usage already near-universal, the sellable work is **not
  evangelising adoption — it is governing it**. AI governance for universities: use policy,
  traceability, detection, LGPD / Ley 1581 evidence. 🔵 The only region where institutional demand
  *precedes* institutional supply.

### 🔴 Declared regional gaps (a nil in prose reads like coverage)

| region | missing |
|---|---|
| APAC | 2026 player ranking; per-country education policy; classroom data |
| North America | primary source for the $169 M commitment; concrete state education-AI rules |
| EMEA | authoritative 2026 vendor list; **applied** high-risk AI Act practice in education |
| LATAM | 2026 national education-specific AI regulation; K-12 deployment data |

## 🆕 p592 — 2026-11-01 — the regional legs are saturated at `4 of 4` paid / `0 of 4` new, and the single operative fact they added is that the EU high-risk clock no longer waits for standards

### 🔴 The demand-side legs, measured against this base rather than described

All four mandated regional legs ran in full. 🔵 **Every distinctive token each one served was
already held by this base**, so the honest headline is *saturation*, not nil: the legs are
working and the base has caught up with them.

| mandated regional leg | distinctive tokens served | already held | 🟢 new to this base |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `AI education North America 2026 adoption regulation players` | `134` bills / `31` states; `35+` states with guidance; NC Session Law `2026-41` and its three dates; Maryland's package; SUNY AI-literacy gen-ed from autumn `2026`; UC Berkeley Law genAI prohibition; ED grant-priority rule; the student-authored *STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026* | `8` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| `AI education EMEA Europe Middle East Africa 2026 adoption regulation players` | EU AI Act Annex III high-risk status; Digital Omnibus deferral to `2 December 2027` / `2 August 2028`; Article 50 live from `2 August 2026`; KHDA Dubai guidance; UNESCO generative-AI guidance; OECD *Digital Education Outlook 2026*; European market `$2.64 bn` | `7` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| `AI education APAC Asia Pacific 2026 adoption regulation players` | Korea AI Basic Act (`22 January 2026`); Vietnam AI law (`1 March 2026`) and its `18`-month education transition; Ipsos *Education Monitor 2026* ban-support figures; Japan's voluntary posture; Australia's National AI Plan; China's targeted measures | `6` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| `AI education LATAM Latin America 2026 adoption regulation players` | UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS (`200` institutions, `19` countries, `87 %` use, `26 %` strategy); Universidad de los Andes `716` instruments across `23` jurisdictions, `>80 %` since 2023; UNESCO Observatory launched `14 April 2026` in Santiago with CAF, CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC, Ceibal, Santillana, Tec de Monterrey, ProFuturo, IRCAI | `3` | 🔴 **`0`** |

🔵 **`24` distinctive tokens, `24` already held.** `p588` declared these legs saturated at
`20 of 24` and `p591` measured `15 of 16`; this pass is the first to run them and recover
**nothing at all**, which — read together with `592-A` on
[`../agents/trending.md`](../agents/trending.md) — is what three months of pointing the
rotation at a single industry looks like from the demand side.

### 🟢 The one operative fact this pass adds: the EU high-risk clock is decoupled from standards

🆕 **The deferred Annex III and Annex I dates apply on the calendar, whether or not the
supporting harmonised standards are finished.** This differs from the Commission's original
omnibus proposal, which would have tied the start of high-risk obligations to the
*availability* of those standards. 🔵 **Why this is operationally different from a date
slipping:** a standards-linked trigger is an open-ended extension a vendor can plan to sit
behind; a fixed date is a deadline that arrives on an education product whether the
conformity toolkit exists or not. 🔴 **For a studio shipping grading, admissions or
proctoring into the EU, `2 December 2027` should therefore be planned as firm**, and the
conformity-assessment work scoped without assuming a standards-driven reprieve.

🟡 **Provenance, stated plainly:** this is a **secondary** reading, carried by the European
Parliament's legislative-train entry for the Digital Omnibus on AI and by one law-firm
commentary. 🔴 Both were served through `WebSearch` result text; `www.europarl.europa.eu`
returned **`ENOTFOUND`** to a direct fetch in this environment, so the wording of the
operative article has **not** been read here.

### 🟡 `586-E` — not closed, but the corroboration count is now six and the spread is gone

| element | value now corroborated | sources agreeing this pass |
|---|---|--:|
| Instrument | **Regulation (EU) 2026/1744**, OJ **`24 July 2026`**, in force **`27 July 2026`** | `2` |
| Stand-alone Annex III high-risk (education, grading, admissions) | **`2 December 2027`** — a `16`-month deferral from `2 August 2026` | `6` |
| Annex I product-embedded high-risk | **`2 August 2028`** — a `12`-month deferral from `2 August 2027` | `3` |
| Article 50 transparency | 🔴 **not deferred** — applies from **`2 August 2026`** | `2` |
| Article 50(2) machine-readable marking, systems already on the market | **`2 December 2026`** backstop | `1` |

🟢 **The `16`-month spread `586-E` was opened over is no longer a disagreement between
sources.** Six independent professional sources — a cloud-security research note, Orrick,
DLA Piper, Pinsent Masons, a consent-management vendor's knowledge hub, and a dedicated AI
Act tracker — now all give **`2 December 2027`** for stand-alone Annex III. The earlier
*"August 2026"* readings are **superseded guides written before the omnibus**, not a live
contest. 🔴 **The gap stays OPEN regardless**, and the reason is a channel limit, not a
doubt: `eur-lex.europa.eu`, `digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu` and `www.europarl.europa.eu` are
all unreachable from this environment, so **no primary text has been read**. 🔵 Closing it
requires one Official Journal read from a network that can reach the EU domains — it does
not require more search.

### 🟢 North America

🔴 **Nil new.** The leg reconfirmed the state-level picture this base already holds: `134`
AI-in-education bills across `31` states in the 2026 session, `35+` state education
departments with published guidance, and North Carolina's Session Law `2026-41` staging a
state model policy by **`2026-12-31`**, local school-unit policies by **`2027-06-30`** and
teacher professional development by **`2028-06-30`**. 🔵 Federal action remains indirect —
an ED rule prioritising grant applications that advance understanding or ethical use of AI.
🟢 Higher education is where the datable commitments are: **SUNY** requires AI literacy in
general education for undergraduates entering from autumn `2026`; **UC Berkeley School of
Law** prohibits generative AI for most graded work from summer `2026`. 🔵 **The two postures
are opposite and both are procurement signals** — a system-wide literacy mandate buys
curriculum and assessment work, a prohibition buys detection and academic-integrity work.

### 🟢 EMEA

🟢 **The EU remains the structural fact and it is now a fixed-date one** — see the
decoupling finding above. Education uses sit in Annex III, enforcement machinery has been
live since `2 August 2026`, and the compliance clock runs to `2 December 2027`.
🔵 Gulf policy stays government-led, with Dubai's **KHDA** issuing explicit responsible-AI
guidance to private schools. 🔵 **OECD's *Digital Education Outlook 2026*** recommends moving
past general-purpose assistants toward purpose-built educational AI — which is the same
movement [`./trends.md`](./trends.md) tracks on the supply side.

🔴 **AFRICA IS A DECLARED GAP, AND IT IS A MEASURED ABSENCE RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** The
mandated EMEA leg names Africa in the query string and returned **no substantive coverage of
African AI-in-education regulation or adoption at all** — not a thin result, a nil. 🔵 This
base's two standing African leads, an **AUDA-NEPAD** guideline and the **AU Continental
Education Strategy**, remain unverifiable here: `www.nepad.org` is among the hosts returning
no connection. 🔴 **So EMEA coverage in this base is in practice EU + Gulf**, and a
Sub-Saharan engagement would start without regional intelligence. Closing this needs a
channel that reaches African IGO domains, or a named local source — not another run of the
same leg, which has now failed to serve Africa across multiple passes.

### 🟢 APAC

🔴 **Nil new.** Korea's **AI Basic Act** (in force `22 January 2026`, the region's first
comprehensive AI statute) and **Vietnam's** AI law (effective `1 March 2026`, with a
**`18`-month** transition for systems already operating in education, health and finance)
are both already held, as are Japan's light-touch voluntary model, Australia's reliance on
existing law under its National AI Plan, and China's targeted measures on generative
services and AI-content labelling. 🔵 The **Ipsos Education Monitor 2026** attitude figures
are also held, and they move *against* adoption: support for banning AI in schools rose from
`21 %` to `29 %` in Japan, `47 %` to `52 %` in Australia and `27 %` to `31 %` in South
Korea, with Indonesia lowest at `23 %`. 🔴 **Public opinion in APAC is hardening while the
statutes are liberalising** — a delivery risk that belongs in an APAC proposal, not a
market-size slide.

### 🟢 LATAM

🔴 **Nil new**, and the region's defining asymmetry is unchanged: **adoption is near-universal
and governance is not.** UNESCO **IESALC**/UNU-IAS, across `200` institutions in `19`
countries — **`87 %`** report using AI in at least one area, **`26 %`** have a formal AI
strategy, and uptake is bottom-up, driven by individual academics, researchers and students
rather than institutional plans. 🔵 Regulation is voluminous and fragmented: Universidad de
los Andes counts **`716`** AI-related instruments, rules and proposals across `23`
jurisdictions, **over `80 %`** initiated since 2023. 🟢 The institutional counterpart is the
**UNESCO Observatory on AI in Education for Latin America and the Caribbean**, launched
`14 April 2026` in Santiago with CAF and a named partner set (CENIA, Cetic.br, ECLAC,
Fundação Ceibal, Fundación Santillana, Tecnológico de Monterrey, ProFuturo, IRCAI).
🔴 **The `87 %` / `26 %` pair is the LATAM engagement thesis**: the buying need is
governance, policy and AI-literacy scaffolding over tools institutions have *already*
adopted without a strategy — not greenfield deployment.

🟡 **One figure this base continues to refuse:** a trading blog's claim that Latin America
is the fastest-growing AI-adoption region in 2026 is promotional, unsourced, and is **not**
recorded as a fact here.

### 🔴 The demand-side legs, measured against this base rather than described

🔵 **`p588` declared the regional intel legs saturated at `20 of 24`. This pass puts a

education law.** 🔵 **The scheduling conclusion, stated plainly:** the mandated regional
*demand* legs are a reconfirmation instrument on this industry and should be budgeted as
one. 🟢 They are not worthless — they confirmed nothing has reversed — but a pass that
expects new demand facts from them is mispriced.

### 🟡 The one demand-side reconciliation worth recording

🔴 **The EU high-risk date for education AI is still reported three different ways, and this
pass's sources disagree with each other in the same search.** One guide this pass retrieved
states high-risk obligations *"apply from August 2026"*; a second states the **Digital
Omnibus** amended that to **2 December 2027** for stand-alone Annex III systems and
**2 August 2028** for AI embedded in regulated products; the European Commission's own page
confirms the AI Office and national authorities **began enforcing from 2 August 2026** and
that the amendments entered into force **27 July 2026**. 🟢 **Current position to quote:
the Omnibus dates (Dec 2027 / Aug 2028), with the enforcement machinery already live since
August 2026.** 🔴 **Why this cannot be closed here:** the primary source is the Official
Journal, and `588-B` stands — no non-GitHub primary host is reachable from this harness.
🔵 So this is recorded as *the best secondary reconciliation available*, not as a verified
date, and an engagement must have counsel confirm it.

### 🟢 What this pass DID add: supply-side regional intelligence

🔵 **This base's regional pages describe demand — adoption rates, regulation, budgets —
and have never placed the *supply* a studio would actually deploy.** `p591` measured it:
`37` discovery axes, `45` addresses this base did not hold, `65` grants byte-read
(method and tables on [`../repos/trending.md`](../repos/trending.md) and
[`../agents/top.md`](../agents/top.md)).

| Region | 🟢 adoptable in-language / in-region artefacts added this pass | 🔴 blocked (no grant) | What the number means for an engagement |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| **APAC** | 🟢 **`11`** | `4` | Deep in-language supply across Chinese, Korean and Japanese: study workbenches, national-textbook ingestion, high-stakes exam prep. 🟢 **An APAC engagement can compose rather than build.** |
| **LATAM** | 🟢 `6` | 🔴 **`6`** | 🔴 **The only region where blocked equals adopted.** Courseware licenses, tutors do not — `591-C`. |
| **EMEA** | 🔴 **`2`** | `1` | 🔴 **The thinnest in-language supply of any region this pass** — one German STEM tutor, one Persian subtitling tool. For a continent of `24` official EU languages this is close to nothing. |
| **North America** | `2` | `1` | English-language supply is abundant but is filed `Global`, so the regional count understates it. 🔵 Read NA as served by the `Global` tier. |

🔴 **`591-F` — the EMEA in-language gap is now the largest measured supply gap in this
base, and it is larger than LATAM's.** This base has spent `5` passes on LATAM's
in-language asymmetry (`p587`'s `33×`) and has never measured EMEA's. Measured this pass:
**`2` in-language EMEA artefacts against APAC's `11`, a `5.5×` asymmetry in the same
instrument** — and unlike LATAM, where the supply exists but is unlicensed, in EMEA **the
supply is simply absent**. 🟢 **Those are different commercial situations and need different
answers:** a LATAM engagement negotiates or rebuilds six known tutors; an EMEA engagement
has nothing in-language to negotiate for and must localise from the `Global` tier. 🔵 This
is the measurement to re-run next pass with EMEA-language free-text axes (German, French,
Spanish-of-Spain, Italian, Polish, Dutch, Arabic, Turkish), which **no pass has run** —
`p576`'s native-language legs were per-*jurisdiction regulation*, not per-language supply.

### 🔵 Regional demand snapshots — restated, with provenance, so nothing above reads as new

#### 🟢 North America

Posture unchanged: **state-led, no federal statute**, restrictive rather than enabling.
This pass reconfirmed the `134`-bill/`31`-state volume figure (🔴 retired — do not price
against it), the **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act of 2026** at committee stage,
**Maryland**'s guidance-plus-local-policy package, **New York**'s proposal to limit
classroom AI to grade 9 and above, Gallup's ~`60 %` teacher adoption, and North America at
`36 %` of global market share. 🔴 **New facts: nil.** 🟡 One token arrived that this base
holds once — Canada's **Working for Workers Four Act, 2024** — and it is **employment law,
not education law**; recorded so it is not re-filed as a Canadian education instrument.

#### 🟢 EMEA

**EU:** enforcement machinery live since **2 August 2026** (AI Office + national
authorities); high-risk obligations for education deferred by the **Digital Omnibus** to
**2 December 2027** (stand-alone Annex III) and **2 August 2028** (embedded in regulated
products); amendments in force **27 July 2026**. 🔴 Sources disagree — see the
reconciliation above. **UK:** light-touch, principle-based through existing regulators,
consultation ongoing. **Gulf:** Dubai's **KHDA** has issued explicit responsible-AI
guidance to private schools (🟡 thinly held, secondary, commercial-blog source). **EU
uptake policy:** the **AI Continent Action Plan** names education among the sectors to
accelerate (🟡 held in only `2` files — the thinnest token this pass served).
🔴 **Reliable EMEA *adoption* statistics were not returned by this leg** — a declared nil,
and the second consecutive pass to declare it. 🔴 **And see `591-F`: EMEA is also this
base's thinnest region for adoptable in-language supply.**

#### 🟢 APAC

**Korea:** AI Basic Act in force, high-impact duties (transparency, risk assessment, human
oversight, documentation) from **22 January 2026**, with a guidance-first grace period
through 2026. **Vietnam:** law **134/2025/QH15** effective **1 March 2026**, risk-based,
and **education is a named high-risk sector with automated assessment called out
explicitly** — the most directly product-relevant APAC obligation in this base.
**Australia:** **TEQSA** requires *all* higher-education providers to file institutional
genAI action plans (🟡 thinly held). **Singapore:** consulting on AI Risk Management
Guidelines. **Indonesia:** rules expected as a Presidential Regulation. **China:**
mandatory registration and content-labelling. **Japan:** light-touch/voluntary.
🟢 **APAC is also the region with the deepest adoptable supply this pass (`11`)**, so it is
the one region where regulation is tightening *and* composition is cheap.

#### 🟢 LATAM

**Adoption is near-universal and governance is not.** UNESCO **IESALC** 2026: `87 %` of
institutions in Latin America and the Caribbean use AI in at least one area, with adoption
driven by faculty, researchers and students **rather than institutional policy**. **Digital
Education Council** 2026 LATAM survey (30,000+ responses, 29 institutions, with
Tecnológico de Monterrey): `92 %` of students and `79 %` of faculty actively engaging with
AI, and students asking for clearer governance, citing data privacy and fairness in
AI-based assessment. An October 2026 index of 20 economies places **Brazil, Chile and
Uruguay** in the leading group, notes more than half have AI in school curricula, and finds
**only Brazil and Colombia allocate budget** inside their national instruments. Against
`~70 %` of institutions in Europe and North America having or developing guidance, LAC sits
at `45 %`. 🔴 **New facts: nil — all five figures were already held.** 🟢 **What is new is
`591-C`:** the region's in-language *supply* exists, and `6 of 6` of its conversational AI
tutors carry no licence.

## p587 — 2026-11-01 — the LATAM leg returns a measured `33×` asymmetry against APAC in the same instrument, and the regional market legs are reconciled against a spread this page already publishes

### 🟢 The region finding that is actionable: LATAM has no in-language supply to adopt

The repository-level in-language leg — the channel `p586` used to place its APAC rows —
run in Spanish and Portuguese, with a Chinese control in the same hour:

| leg | query | repos | best row |
|---|---|--:|---|
| LATAM | `educación ia stars:>20` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `tutor educacion stars:>10` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `educacao inteligencia artificial stars:>5` | **0** | — |
| LATAM | `educacao ia stars:>10` | **1** | `kaledbarreto/SophIA` — 15★, **no licence**, last push `2024-05-12` |
| LATAM | `escuela ia stars:>10` | **1** | `maxfloresv/FM849` — 14★, `NOASSERTION`, a **course repo** |
| 🟢 APAC control | `教育 AI stars:>10` | **67** | — |
| 🟢 APAC control | `教育 智能 stars:>20` | **42** | — |

🔴 **Two Iberian-language repositories above 10★ and neither is adoptable, against 67 in the
Chinese-language control — a ~`33×` asymmetry measured with one instrument in one hour.**
The 104 hits on `educacion inteligencia artificial` are coursework repositories at 1–2★
with no licence file.

🔵 **What follows for delivery, concretely.** An APAC engagement can **adopt in-language**
(`kaogong-skill`, `cram-engine`, `cn-math-taxonomy`, `OpenMAIC`, `DeepTutor` are all on
this base's shelves and all APAC-origin). A LATAM engagement **cannot** — it is a
localisation of a Global/English shelf row, or a BUILD. 🟢 That is not a lament: it is the
estimate line item that separates a LATAM proposal from an APAC one, and it is now measured
rather than assumed.

🟡 **Method caveat, quantified:** `educación` returns **5,929** and `educacion` **11,175** —
the unaccented form finds **1.9×** more. An accented-only query under-reports by half, so
both forms are required. (This base holds accent-control evidence from `p476`–`p477`; this
is the figure at a star floor.)

### 🟡 The regional market legs — what they returned, and why no figure here is new

All four mandated regional legs ran. 🔴 **Not one returned a figure this page can adopt
without widening its own published spread**, which `p583` already set at `2.6×` and `p584`
at `3.0×`:

| region | what the leg returned | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| North America | 36–38 % of global share; **$3.68 B (2026)** on a 36 % basis; a third source gives ~$951 M (2024) | 🔴 **Inconsistent with itself** — a 36 % share and a $951 M regional total cannot both hold. Not adopted. |
| EMEA | **56 %** of EMEA CEOs report AI readiness vs **48 %** Americas (Workday); **94 %** likely to invest in AI training in 2026 (CompTIA) | 🟡 **Enterprise-wide, not education.** Directional only, and labelled as such. |
| APAC | **$591.6 M (2024) → $1,848.1 M (2029), 20.9 % CAGR** (MarketsandMarkets) | 🟢 **The only internally consistent regional series the leg returned**, and the slowest CAGR on this page — notable against the 41 %+ global figures. |
| LATAM | *"Rest of Latin America"* **$18.2 M (2024) → ~$36 M (2029)** | 🔴 **Scope-incoherent**: "Rest of" excludes Brazil and Mexico, so it is not a LATAM total and must never be quoted as one. |

🔵 **The repo-level LATAM finding above is worth more than any of these four figures**,
because it was measured first-hand rather than purchased, and it changes an estimate.

### 🟢 Regulatory movement by region, dated

- **North America** — **134 bills across 31 states** tracked on AI in education as of
  **March 2026** (privacy, classroom use, curriculum); **$169 M** in US Department of
  Education awards released **January 2026** including responsible-AI-in-higher-ed support.
  🔴 Still no federal statute: the posture remains state-by-state, which is what makes
  `canvas-mcp`-style **attachment** rather than forking the right architecture here.
- **EMEA** — EU AI Act **AI-literacy obligations in force since `2025-02-02`**; the European
  Commission has **updated its ethical guidelines on AI in education**; the UK's first **AI
  Adoption Summit** committed **£200 m+**. 🔴 The Annex III high-risk compliance date
  remains contested across EU-facing sources (see the `p586` section) — unchanged this pass.
- **APAC** — 🔴 **Forrester: no common APAC-wide AI law is expected**, and the 2026 regional
  frame is **sovereignty, security and responsible deployment**. 🟢 This is why the APAC
  shelf rows that run **offline or on local weights** (`wordlink`'s fine-tuned 1.5B,
  `Kolibri`) are the strategically valuable ones, not the API-bound tutors.
- **LATAM** — a **UNESCO IESALC / UNU-IAS working paper (`2026-09-01`, Arianna Valentini)**
  maps AI use across LAC universities on five axes; **ILIA 2025** (ECLAC/CEPAL) published
  **2026**; the **AI in Higher Education LATAM Survey 2026** (Tec de Monterrey, February
  2026) reports the region as *more positive about AI in higher education than any other*.
  🔵 **Enthusiasm high, in-language open-source supply ~zero** — those two facts sit
  together and together they describe the opportunity.
