### 🟢 Market sizing — unchanged, and still vendor-only at six passes

**USD 10.6 bn (2026) → USD 42.48 bn (2030)** reproduced again from the ResearchAndMarkets
family; HolonIQ's **USD 12.3 bn** and Emerline's **>USD 12.5 bn** also reproduced for 2026.
🔴 **No independent estimate has been located in six passes. Quote the range, never a
point**, and attribute it. One widely repeated figure — `83 %` of institutions planning an
AI teaching-assistant deployment, attributed to EDUCAUSE — **could not be verified** and
should not be used.

### (historical) Opportunities by region — superseded, retained for history

### North America

🟢 **The buyer is a state education department or a university system, and the purchase is
a guardrail, not a capability.** `134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states in 2026
converge on three asks: human oversight, no AI in high-stakes student decisions, and AI
literacy. **SUNY** (`64` campuses) approved a systemwide AI policy in spring 2026 and
requires AI-literacy training inside general education for incoming undergraduates from
**fall 2026** — that is the shape of the budgeted work.
🟢 **Dated compliance deadlines now exist, which makes this a procurement conversation:**
**Ohio** requires districts to adopt AI-use policies by **`1 July 2026`**; **North
Carolina** runs staged deadlines **through 2028**; **California AB 1159** bars the use of
student data to train AI models; **Florida**'s Board of Governors is weighing a rule
obliging every public college to hold an AI policy — **only two, including the University
of Florida, currently have one**, which is the gap a studio sells into.
🔴 **Correction to this page's standing caveat.** It has read *"federal action is thin; do
not build a pitch on anticipated federal regulation."* **That is now too strong:** a
White House national AI policy framework released **`20 March 2026`** urges Congress to
set a unified federal approach that **would preempt much of the state activity** above.
🔵 **The planning consequence is real:** the `134`-bill patchwork that justifies a
state-by-state compliance engagement is the same patchwork a federal preemption would
collapse. **Scope North American compliance work so it survives preemption** — build the
oversight and audit capability, not fifty policy documents.
🔵 Institutional hard lines are appearing too: **Berkeley Law** banned AI for all exams
and credited coursework effective **summer 2026**. Expect per-faculty divergence inside
single universities.

### EMEA

🟢 **The strongest-evidenced region, the only one where the compliance clock is dated, and
now the one with the best-measured *implementation* failure.** Annex III obligations for
stand-alone education systems from **`2 December 2027`**, embedded products
**`2 August 2028`**, Article 50 transparency **`2 August 2026`** — grading, applicant
screening and exam supervision explicitly in scope.
🔴 **Germany is the quantified case for why compliance work is billable.** A **DFKI** and
**mmb Institut** analysis counts **`56` separate rule-sets** governing AI in German
schools, **`16` of them from the Länder alone**. The **Deutsche Telekom Stiftung**
Trendmonitor 2026 finds AI use in schools rose sharply in a single year, while a **Forsa**
survey of ~`1,000` teachers finds the large majority have **barely or never engaged with
the rules that apply to them**. 🟢 **Adoption without comprehension, across 56
overlapping frameworks, is a governance product with a named buyer.** The **KMK** added AI
competencies to its *Bildung in der digitalen Welt* framework on **`16 October 2026`**
without settling teaching hours, year groups or exam formats — so the implementation layer
is unspecified and therefore commissionable. Divergence is concrete: **Hessen** requires
AI use *including prompts* to be labelled; **NRW** requires full disclosure of all aids;
**Bavaria** moved by cabinet decision in June 2026 to stop universities blanket-banning AI
in unsupervised exams.
🟢 **France is the most prescriptive and therefore the most predictable.** The ministry's
*cadre d'usage de l'IA en éducation* (June 2025) covers teachers, students and
administrative staff, **forbids entering identifiable student data into non-approved
tools**, and requires any AI use in submitted work to be declared; unauthorised generative
AI in an assignment is **formally fraud**. A *Bulletin officiel* note of
**`5 February 2026`** introduces the **Pix IA** pathway for *4e*, *seconde* and first-year
*CAP* students, phased across 2025-26 and 2026-27.
🟢 **Spain is the live near-term opening.** The Ministry of Science, Innovation and
Universities is preparing a *real decreto* on university teaching containing, **for the
first time, a dedicated section on classroom AI**, negotiated with **CRUE**; the stated
intent is that **every university must hold an ethical/deontological code covering
faculty, students, the institution and research**. 🟢 **That is a mandated artefact with a
deadline and no incumbent supplier — the cleanest EMEA engagement on this page.**
🔴 **Figures not to quote.** Spanish adoption is contradictory across sources (`72 %` vs
`61 %` of public universities having integrated AI into at least one degree, the latter
attributed to a CRUE 2025-26 report); a French *"€230 M / 12,000 establishments"* programme
and a *"90 % of collèges and lycées"* claim are **single-source and the second is not
credible**. The high-risk timetable is itself contested — one line of sources gives August
2026, another points to the **Digital Omnibus** having deferred it. **Quote the Article 50
date; hedge the Annex III date.**
🟢 Post-market monitoring remains the build, and the components are EMEA-origin:
`ing-bank/popmon` (MIT, `512★`, ING Bank N.V.) and
`IFCA-Advanced-Computing/frouros` (BSD-3-Clause, `260★`, Instituto de Física de Cantabria).
🔵 Watch the **Council of Europe** Legal Instrument on AI in Education and its companion
**common repository for evaluating AI's effectiveness** — two-source, still secondary.
🔴 **Primary EU text remains unreadable from this session** (`eur-lex.europa.eu` does not
resolve); every date here is secondary. The `41 %`-of-European-VC-funds claim is **not** to
be quoted.

### APAC

🟢 **The region supplying the technology, which is the asymmetry to exploit.** Most of
this pass's new permissive rows are APAC-origin, and the field's only frontier-scale
education agent, `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (Apache-2.0, **`52,957★`**, roughly doubled since this
base's cycle-3 measurement), is APAC. 🟢 **An engagement here starts from a stronger local
component base than anywhere else, and the components are permissive** —
`modelscope/FunASR` (MIT), `QwenAudio/SenseVoice` (MIT, five languages in one model),
`RapidAI/RapidOCR` (Apache-2.0, CPU-deployable), `breezedeus/Pix2Text` (MIT, maths OCR),
`oomol-lab/pdf-craft` (MIT, scanned-textbook digitisation).
🟢 **China is the largest state-directed programme on this page.** The MOE, NDRC, MIIT,
MOST and the National Data Bureau jointly issued the **"AI + Education" Action Plan**
(《"人工智能+教育"行动计划》, 教科信〔2026〕1号) in **April 2026**: it extends the
*General AI Literacy Education Guide for Primary and Secondary Schools*, pushes AI into
local curricula, and — the part that matters commercially — requires a **classified,
tiered security-assurance regime** (分类分级) for AI in education. Delivery scaffolding is
already named: pilots across **7 eastern provinces, 20 central/western cities and 18
universities**, plus **509 national AI-education base schools**. Earlier instruments cover
student, vocational and teacher genAI use separately. 🔵 Sources disagree on whether the
plan issued `2` or `10 April 2026`.
🔴 **Korea is this pass's most important negative signal, and it is a reversal.** The
flagship **AI digital textbook** (AI 디지털교과서) programme has been **reclassified from
"textbook" (교과서) to "educational material" (교육자료)** — losing its mandatory force.
The ministry now speaks of **"AI 교육자료"**, framed as autonomous selection with guidance
on selection criteria, and **several provincial offices cut or deferred their 2026 AIDT
subscription budgets**. The original roadmap (grades 3–4, M1 and H1 in 2025; grades 5–6
and M2 plus Korean, social studies, science and technology-home-economics in 2026) is
therefore no longer a procurement schedule. 🟢 **What survives is still fundable:**
K-education AI development and **`1,000` AI-focused schools** (AI 중점학교).
🔵 **Read this as the pattern, not the exception:** a national mandate can be downgraded
to an option mid-rollout. **Do not price an APAC engagement off a mandate's existence.**
🟢 **Japan is the clearest "unspecified layer" opportunity.** The operative MEXT text is
still the generative-AI guideline **Ver. 2.0 of `26 December 2024`**, structured around
teacher administrative use, student learning activities and board-of-education response.
A revision to **Ver. 2.1** was tabled at the Digital Learning Infrastructure Special
Committee on **`30 June 2026`** and, as of late 2026, **has not been published**. Its
declared scope is agentic AI, international practice, and — notably — **excessive
short-circuiting of the learning process (認知的オフロード, cognitive offload)**. 🔵 A
guideline explicitly worried about cognitive offload is a guideline that will ask vendors
to *evidence learning*, not engagement. Build the measurement.
🟢 **India is the largest volume play, with a dated curriculum trigger.** The Ministry of
Education has announced an **AI and Computational Thinking curriculum from Class 3,
beginning the 2026-27 session**, aligned to **NEP 2020** and **NCF-SE 2023**, with an
expert committee led by an **IIT Madras** professor designing it and **NCERT** reviewing.
CBSE already runs AI as a Class IX elective (since 2019-20) and a 15-hour skill module
from Class VI; the UGC added AI and ML to undergraduate curricula in 2022. 🔴 **The
governance hole is explicit:** as of early 2026 there is **no comprehensive policy from
the UGC, CBSE or state departments on AI misuse in academic submissions**, and the UGC has
pushed the obligation down to individual institutions. 🟢 **Thousands of institutions each
needing their own academic-integrity policy and detection posture is a repeatable product.**
🔵 A **₹500 crore** AI-for-education Centre of Excellence is cited without an official
notification — do not quote. Sources also disagree on whether the Class 3 rollout is
mandatory or still being designed.
🔵 Standing regional regulation, unchanged: Korea's **AI Basic Act** in force
`22 January 2026`, **Vietnam** from `1 March 2026` (18-month education transition),
Japan and Australia light-touch, Singapore voluntary (**AI Verify**).

### LATAM

🟢 **The best-documented adoption-versus-governance gap in the world — and as of this pass
it has a dated statutory anchor, which it never had before.** `87 %` of surveyed
institutions use AI in at least one area; `26 %` have a formal framework; **`55 %` have no
dedicated AI budget**. Ceibal reports `75 %` of Uruguayan public-school teachers using
these tools; Cetic.br reports `53 %` of Brazilian administrators using AI with only `22 %`
of schools holding guidelines.
🟢 **Brazil now has the region's first national regulation of AI in education.** The
**CNE** approved a *parecer* (reported as **CNE/CP nº 15/2026**, approved around
**`1 September 2026`**) covering schools and universities, with homologation by the
Education Minister (**Leonardo Barchini**) reported for the week of **`13 October 2026`**
and **12 months for institutions to comply** from homologation. Its provisions are
unusually specific and they are **product requirements**:
- 🟢 AI may act as a **first filter** on essays and open-response questions, but **may not
  determine pass or fail** — the tool must be built to *refuse* the terminal decision.
- 🔴 **Total prohibition of AI in early-childhood education** (*educação infantil*), even
  teacher-mediated.
- 🟢 AI tools are classified across **four risk levels**.
- 🟢 Universities must write AI-mediation **teacher training** into their **PDI and PPC**
  institutional plans — a mandated, dated, recurring deliverable.

🟢 Alongside it, MEC published a *referencial* on responsible AI use and development in
education (Mar–Apr 2026) which institutionalises digital-education guidance and promotes
**"IA Desplugada"** — teaching AI concepts **without computers**, designed for
low-connectivity schools. 🔵 **That is a genuinely LATAM-shaped requirement and a
differentiator**: a curriculum product that degrades to paper.
🔴 **Mexico and Colombia are the contrast, and the gap is the opportunity.** Neither has a
general AI law. **Mexico** regulates sectorally and sub-nationally — the **Estado de
México** approved a reform in **April 2026** promoting responsible, gradual AI use in
upper-secondary and higher education, and a **federal initiative of April 2026** would
amend the *Ley General de Educación* on the explicit grounds that the system **"lacks
specific provisions regulating the incorporation, use, scope and limits of AI in teaching
and learning"**; it is a proposal only. There is **no national public standard for the
minimum AI competency of a serving teacher**, against a stated need to train **more than
one million** of them. 🟢 **Teacher AI-capability certification at national scale, with no
incumbent standard, is the single largest LATAM engagement visible on this page.**
**Colombia** rests on **CONPES 4144** — a policy document, not a law (six axes, horizon
2030, ~COP `479` bn) — and has **no binding Ministry of Education guidance on classroom
use**; its AI bill (`043/2025` Senate / `324/2025` Chamber) was still in first debate in
May 2026 despite presidential urgency.
🔵 **Only Peru and El Salvador had AI-specific legislation fully in force as of July
2026**; Colombia, Brazil, Chile and Mexico were at different legislative stages.
🟢 Regional technical credibility exists and is shelved: `douglasrizzo/catsim`
(BSD-3-Clause, `153★`) is a Brazil-maintained adaptive-testing simulator.
🔵 Named counterparties: **UNESCO IESALC**, the **Observatory on AI in Education for LAC**
(`14 April 2026`, Santiago), **Ceibal**, **Cetic.br**, **CENIA**, **CAF**, **ECLAC**,
**Tec de Monterrey**, **ProFuturo**, **IRCAI**.
🔴 `716` instruments across `23` jurisdictions, `>80 %` since 2023: design for
jurisdictional variation, do not bet on one statute.

### Global

🔵 **Cross-regional, not region-less.** The licence structure holds everywhere: **both
LMSs of record are copyleft** (Open edX AGPL-3.0, Moodle GPL-3.0) and **there is no
permissive student-information system anywhere**. The portable play is the same in
every region: build proprietary AI components against permissive extension points
rather than forking a platform.
🟢 **This pass adds a second portable layer, and it is bigger than the platform question.**
Document ingestion — `microsoft/markitdown` (MIT, `193,266★`), `run-llama/liteparse`
(Apache-2.0), `The-Vibe-Company/megaparse` (Apache-2.0) — is region-neutral, permissive,
and the prerequisite for every AI feature an institution actually asks for. **No
jurisdiction regulates format conversion**, so it is the one component that ships
unchanged everywhere.
🔴 **A constraint that is now global rather than regional:** three of the five large
jurisdictions examined this pass (Brazil, France, Germany) independently require that AI
**not** make the terminal assessment decision and that its use be **declared or labelled**.
🟢 **Build human-in-the-loop refusal and provenance logging as defaults, not options** —
it is the same requirement in four regulatory languages.

(`1 March 2026`, with an `18`-month transition for education against `12` general);

jurisdiction's own language (`p575`, `p576`) — was not re-run this pass; the pass spent its
budget on the supply side instead, and that is where both findings came from.

### 🟢 The pass's supply-side result: a first-hand star-and-licence channel this base had registered as unreachable

🟢 **`Gap 464-D` and `Gap 505-B` are refuted.** Both record the GitHub **search** API as
`403` from this session, and the figure register concluded a first-hand star count was
**"still none"**. Four invocation forms were tried this pass and all four returned `200`,
including the exact inline form `464-D` cites and including plain `curl` with no `gh` and
no token. 🔴 The `403` is real but belongs to **`/repos/{owner}/{repo}`** — the per-repo
endpoint — which had been collapsed into the same gap. Search carries `license.spdx_id`,
`stargazers_count`, `pushed_at` and `archived` for any public repository at **30 calls a
minute**; `47` addresses were re-measured with it this pass, and eight ledger licence
entries that stood as `unstated` or two-valued were settled in one call each.

🔵 **Why that is a market fact and not just an instrument fact.** Every licence and star
figure this page quotes to a client had, until this pass, no authoritative channel behind
it. It has one now, and the figures below are measured rather than carried.

### 🔴 Two ledger rows that contradict this page — and are *not* new licence findings

Both licences were read and correctly published passes ago. The defect is in the
machine-readable ledger column, which is what the gates read:

| Row | Ledger column | What these pages already say | Commercial reality |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CaviraOSS/PageLM` — `2,057★` | **`MIT`** | `BLOCK` / `REFUSED`, *"bespoke non-commercial"*, `LICENSE.md` read at `8,563 B`, recorded as *"MIT's body verbatim, then commercial-use, SaaS and resale restrictions"* | 🔴 unusable in a paid deliverable without written permission — **already this page's position** |
| `xiaoyang111222/cn-math-taxonomy` — `5,654★` | **`CC-BY`** | **CC BY-NC 4.0** (`19,347 B`); `Gap 572-C` declares the NonCommercial block on the prerequisite-graph layer | 🔴 NonCommercial — fine for a ministry or non-profit engagement with attribution, unusable commercially. **Already this page's position** |

🔵 **The transferable warning for a procurement conversation is the one this base already
holds and this pass re-confirms:** in education the code is given away and the *content* is
withheld — wordlists, knowledge graphs, item banks, audio. **Read the content licence
before the code licence**, and treat a NonCommercial or source-available grant as the live
risk rather than copyleft, which at least announces itself.

(2026-10-31). Policy items carry a date and an institution. Market-size figures

**The structural commercial shift:** OpenAI's country-level education programme
with eight national partners (Q1 2026) moves buying from school-by-school sales
toward national infrastructure deals. That changes the counterparty from a
district to a ministry in every region, and it favours firms that can deliver
compliance documentation alongside software.


## Market size — published as a range, because the sources disagree

**USD 10.6–12.5 bn (2026), growing to USD 42.5 bn by 2030 on a ~41% CAGR.**

Every figure in that range is a market-research or vendor output. HolonIQ puts
2026 at **USD 12.3 bn**; another 2026 roundup claims the market "surged past the
**USD 12.5 bn** milestone"; earlier cycles of this KB recorded **USD 9.58–10.6
bn** from other vendors. **No independent estimate has been located in three
passes.** Quote the range to a client, never a point. The 2030 figure and the
CAGR come from the same vendor family as the 2026 figures and inherit their
uncertainty.

Two widely circulated figures are **deliberately excluded**: an "83% of
institutions plan AI teaching assistant deployments by end of 2026" attributed
to EDUCAUSE (not traceable to EDUCAUSE in any source seen across three passes),
and "AI agents will take over 80% of routine educational labour" (unsupported).

## 🆕 `p576` — 2026-10-31 — 🟢 **`575-E` CLOSED ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT: SIX LATAM JURISDICTIONS HAVE DATED EDUCATION-AI INSTRUMENTS, AND COLOMBIA'S IS A STATUTE — AFTER THIRTEEN PASSES IN WHICH THE REGION-LEVEL LEG REACHED NONE. 🔴 AND THE REGION'S LOUDEST SECONDARY CLAIM, "AI IS ALREADY MANDATORY IN ARGENTINE SCHOOLS", IS REFUTED AT THE LEVEL IT WAS STATED.**

🟢 **The prescription worked, and that is now twice in two passes.** `p575` broke a
twelve-pass APAC nil by querying **per jurisdiction, in the jurisdiction's language**
(trend 23). This pass ran the identical method on LATAM, where the same leg had failed
**thirteen** times, and reached six jurisdictions on the first attempt. The full table,
with grades and binding/non-binding marked per row, is in
[`../evidence/latam-jurisdiction-instruments.2026-10-31-p576.tsv`](../evidence/latam-jurisdiction-instruments.2026-10-31-p576.tsv).

🔵 **Why this belongs on a market page and not only a policy one:** a curricular mandate
is a *procurement trigger with a date*. Colombia's statute obliges a subject to be taught
that most of its teachers were never trained to teach, in a country whose own open-source
supply in its own language tops out at `71★` (measured this pass — see below). That gap is
the engagement.

### 🟢 The one binding instrument: Colombia, `Ley 2626 de 2026`

🟢 **`Ley 2626 de 2026`, the "Ley de Educación Digital", expedida `24 August 2026`** —
🟢 **the first binding education-AI instrument in LATAM on these shelves**, and the first
anywhere on them that works by **curricular mandate** rather than by risk regulation. Read
at article level through a domain-restricted search (`576-B`), it:

- updates **Tecnología e Informática** in *básica* and *media* to carry **computational
  thinking, programming, artificial intelligence, data science and digital citizenship**;
- declares digital education **part of the fundamental right to a quality education**;
- orders a **malla curricular obligatoria, progresiva y articulada por ciclos**;
- obliges **MEN** to review the subject's lineamientos and standards **at least every three
  years**, with a **Comisión Nacional de Educación Digital**;
- creates an **Observatorio Nacional de Educación Digital** to publish implementation and
  gap data — 🔵 **a government-published measurement surface, which is unusual and is
  directly useful to a bidder**;
- carries an **ideological-neutrality clause** on content, a **teacher training and updating
  programme**, and **priority for rural and ethnic-community schools**;
- is 🔴 **obligatory in official (public) schools**, where the equity constraint bites.

🟡 A separate bill, **`PL 418/2026C`**, would add an article `3A` on responsible AI and
social-media use. **It is in trámite and is not law** — recorded so the two are not merged.

### 🔴 The refutation: Argentina

🔴 **Secondary sources state that "AI is already mandatory in Argentine schools." It does
not hold at the national level.** The federal instrument is a **bill** amending art. `11`
of **Ley 26.206**; **no Consejo Federal de Educación resolution number could be found in
two searches**, and the one source asserting a CFE approval gives none. 🟢 **What is real is
sub-national**: **CABA** (AI and digital protection as mandatory content, phased), **Buenos
Aires province** (*IA Generativa en la educación*, `2026-03`, criteria of use for provincial
schools), and **Misiones** (marco orientador with its Secretaría de IA, tied to **Ley VI
Nº 347**). 🔵 **The commercial consequence is concrete: in Argentina the buying unit is the
province or the city, not the nation** — the opposite of Colombia.

### 🔵 The rest of the region, placed

🟡 **Brazil — guidance, not duty, and it is the better-funded of the two tracks.** MEC
published a **documento orientador for educação básica in `April 2026`** — **four pillars**
(critical understanding, intentional pedagogical use, rights protection, professional
development) and **ten diretrizes** for school networks, with a **teacher-training course
opened alongside it**. Behind it sits the **Referencial para o Uso e Desenvolvimento
Responsáveis de Inteligência Artificial na Educação** (**SEGAPE/MEC**), put to **public
consultation in October 2025**, which asks higher-education institutions to stand up **AI
governance guidelines and ethics committees**. 🔵 Neither is binding; both are buyable.

🟡 **Chile — the instrument is a science-ministry policy that assigns work to the education
ministry.** The updated **Política Nacional de Inteligencia Artificial** carries **177
measures**, of which **100 fall due in 2026**, and **Mineduc owns the update of the
curricular bases to address AI from pre-school onward**. Mineduc's Centro de Innovación has
shipped the teacher guide **«PotencIA el Aprendizaje»**, disseminated through *Ciudadanía
Digital* with webinars and regional workshops.

🔴 **Mexico — an informed absence, and this pass corroborated the absence rather than
assuming it.** A DOF-restricted search found **no published AI-in-education acuerdo or
lineamiento**. What exists is the **ENIAG** survey and a **ten-action decálogo** presented
to rectors on **`2026-04-15`**, whose second action asks institutions to set **their own**
lineamientos, with a national higher-education AI policy stated as a *goal*. 🔵 **So in
Mexico the institution is the regulator for now**, which makes institutional governance the
sellable unit. 🟡 One published federal instrument does touch the classroom — a **DOF entry
of `2026-09-22` on responsible device use in schools** — but it is a **phones and personal
electronics rule** that mentions AI mainly for education information systems. Recorded here
so it is not mistaken for an AI rule.

🟡 **Peru** — Minedu announced AI for learning planning in **205 rural schools for 2026**.
🟡 **Dominican Republic** — a ministry statement on teacher training dated **`2026-10-29`**,
with no instrument text reached.

### 🔴 The LATAM market *size* is still not published, and this pass did not fix it

🔴 **`p574` recorded that the available LATAM figure is off by three orders of magnitude.
This pass served it again** — "**USD 18.2 m (2024), 12.1 % CAGR to 2029**" — from a
market-research geography page. 🔵 **A region with `92 %` student engagement and a national
statute cannot have a market two orders of magnitude smaller than a single enterprise
deal.** It stays **unpublished on these shelves**, and the honest line to a client is that
the regional sizing does not exist, not that it is small.

## 🆕 `p575` — 2026-10-31 — 🟢 **`574-D` substantially closed: four APAC jurisdictions have education-specific AI instruments and Korea's is binding**, and every region on this page moved for the first time in thirteen passes

🟢 **The method finding first, because it is the reusable one.** `574-D` recorded that
the mandated **region-level** search leg had failed to reach education-specific
regulation in **twelve consecutive passes**, and prescribed **per-jurisdiction**
queries instead. 🟢 **Run this pass, that prescription worked on the first attempt:**
Korea, Singapore, Japan and Australia all produced dated, education-specific
instruments, and China produced an evidenced absence. 🔵 **The region-level leg is not
the wrong tool because it is broken — it is the wrong *granularity*.** Regulation is
promulgated by jurisdiction, so it must be queried by jurisdiction. Generalises
unchanged to every industry base on this harness.

🔴 **The mandated legs themselves, graded honestly:** the four global legs returned
**`0` new agent addresses (thirteenth consecutive nil)** and re-served the retired
"134 bills / 31 states" figure. The four regional legs returned **`0` new addresses**
— but, unlike the twelve passes before them, **they did pay in regional intelligence**:
the Gallup student-vs-institution split (NA), the Commission's `€25.4 m` and the QS
Europe EdTech 200 (EMEA), and the `92 %` / `79 %` adoption measurement (LATAM).

🟢 **The one binding obligation this pass found:** **Korea's AI Basic Act and
Enforcement Decree took effect `22 January 2026`, and education is a NAMED high-impact
sector** — risk management, explanation, user protection, human oversight and genAI
output labelling, under a `≥ 1`-year MSIT grace period with a proposed delay to 2029
in play. 🔵 **The first binding education-specific AI duty on these shelves outside the
EU AI Act and the US ADA Title II rule.** See `### APAC` below.

#### 🆕 `p576` — 🟢 China closes the last unreached jurisdiction, and Korea's rule narrows to one that can be designed around

🟢 **`575-D` CLOSED. China was reached on the first Chinese-language query** — the query
language was the entire barrier, exactly as trend 23 predicts. The instruments:

- 🟢 **《"人工智能+教育"行动计划》, document number `教科信〔2026〕1号`**, issued by the
  **Ministry of Education with four other departments, April 2026**. It requires localities
  to produce **curriculum guides**, to **define class-hour requirements per school stage**,
  and — 🔵 the commercially sharp clause — to **classify and grade safety-protection
  standards for AI education applications** (`分类分级确定人工智能教育应用的安全防护标准`).
- 🟢 **《中小学生成式人工智能使用指南（2025年版）》** — a **generative-AI use guide for
  primary and secondary students**, the direct analogue of Japan's MEXT guideline.
- 🟢 **《中小学人工智能通识教育指南（2025年版）》** — AI general-education guide, same vintage.
- 🟢 **Revised compulsory-education and senior-secondary Information Technology curriculum
  standards.**
- 🟢 **Two national standards, December 2025, effective on publication:
  《教育数据分类分级指南》** (education data classification and grading) and
  **《大屏幕交互式智能教学终端通用要求》** (general requirements for large-screen interactive
  smart teaching terminals). 🔵 **A hardware conformance standard is a procurement gate**,
  and this base held none from any jurisdiction before now.
- 🟢 **February 2026**, national basic-education work conference: push AI into **curriculum
  standards, daily teaching *and* exam evaluation**.

🔴 **Two honest limits.** Sources describing this as a "compulsory course" are **media
interpretation** — the MOE text speaks of curriculum standards and locally-set class hours,
so do not say "compulsory" to a client. And **no `无障碍` (accessibility) `GB`/`GB/T`
standard applicable to education was reached on a second attempt, now in the right
language** — it stays an informed gap.

### 🔴 Korea: a correction to this base's own claim, and the carve-out that changes the build

🔴 **`p575` published that "education is a NAMED high-impact sector" under the AI Framework
Act. The precise reading is narrower: the named instance is `학생평가` — student
assessment**, given as early-childhood, primary and secondary student evaluation, and it
qualifies only where the system **materially affects life, bodily safety or fundamental
rights**. 🔵 **Not all education AI is high-impact**, and a claim on these shelves is
corrected accordingly.

🟢 **And the Enforcement Decree carries a carve-out this base had not recorded: where a
human intervenes in the final decision, the system is judged controllable and is excluded
from high-impact AI.** 🔵 **That turns compliance into an architecture decision** — a
grading or placement pipeline in which the teacher makes the final call sits outside the
high-impact obligations entirely. **Filed as `P-578-B`**, and it is the most actionable
regulatory finding of this pass.

🟡 **Quantified exposure:** notification duty (**art. `31`**), an administrative fine of up
to **`KRW 30 million`** for failure to pre-notify, and a **watermark / labelling duty on
generative output**. 🟡 An MSIT **amendment to the Enforcement Decree was resolved at Cabinet
on `2026-07-14`** — single source, flagged, not relied on.

🟡 **Japan, now exact:** 「**初等中等教育段階における生成AIの利活用に関するガイドライン
（Ver.2.0）**」, **`2024-12-26`** (provisional Ver.1.0, July 2023). 🔴 It **neither bans nor
mandates** (`一律禁止も義務付けもしない`) — guidance, not duty.

🔴 **Market size: unchanged and still a range, and this pass widened the regional
spread rather than narrowing it.** The NA figures served this pass imply a **`15.9 %`
CAGR** (MarketsandMarkets, through 2029) against this page's **`~41 %`** global CAGR,
which cannot both be true of the same market definition. 🔵 **Quote the range, never a
point** — and for LATAM, quote **no size figure at all** (the available estimate is off
by three orders of magnitude and nothing this pass served corrects it).
## 🆕 `p574` — 2026-10-31 — the `2026` spread is now **2.9×**, and the LATAM figure on offer is off by three orders of magnitude

🔴 **Four 2026 estimates were served by this pass's mandated legs and they do not
reconcile:** IMARC `$6.4B` (for 2025), Persistence `$3.7B` (2026) → `$14.1B`

0.70 bn (2026)** rising to roughly **USD 18.5 bn (2036)**. 🔵 Useful because it
sizes the segment this KB's district-facing work actually sells into, and it shows

🟢 **Regional shares, first time measured on this page:** North America is the
largest region at **~36 %** of the market; **APAC is the fastest-growing at
35.3 % CAGR**. 🔵 This is the quantitative backing for a claim this KB has been
making qualitatively for several passes.


## The structural market fact

The buyable asset in education AI is not the model and not the platform — it is
the **attachment**. Institutions run copyleft platforms of record (Moodle, Open
edX, Canvas, Chamilo) that cannot legally be forked into a proprietary product,
and they run them in exactly the networked way that triggers AGPL. So the
commercial surface is the licence-clean boundary: LTI 1.3, MCP, xAPI. See
[`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).

Two things changed this pass. First, a **permissive AI-native LMS now exists**
(`Selleo/mentingo`, MIT, Poland, 102★), so "substrate replacement" is for the
first time a real option in the corporate-L&D segment, if not yet at university
scale. Second, the agent layer has moved from applications to **agent skills** —
markdown instruction bundles with no runtime — which changes what "delivering an
AI tutor" even means commercially. See [`trends.md`](trends.md).

## Demand-side signal: adoption is ahead of governance everywhere

This is the most robustly evidenced finding in the KB. It now holds with primary
or near-primary evidence on all four regions, which was not true a pass ago.

| Region | Use | Governance | Grade (`569-E`) | Source quality |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LATAM (higher ed) | **87%** of institutions use AI in ≥1 area | **26%** have a formal AI strategy; **55%** have no AI budget; public universities **4%** dedicated funding | 🟢 **A — primary, named instrument**, `n=200`, 19 countries | **Strongest** — UNESCO IESALC with UNU-IAS, 200 institutions, 19 countries |
| EMEA (schools) | **63%** of teachers | ~70% of institutions in Europe/North America have or are developing guidance (UNESCO) | 🔵 **B — two independent surveys agree**; the ~70% figure is region-blended (Europe *and* North America) and so is **not** a clean EMEA count | **Strong** — two independent surveys agree exactly |
| North America (schools) | **60%** of US teachers (Gallup) | Most US public schools still lack a formal student AI policy (Child Trends / Dept of Education) | 🔵 **B — national survey, but `US`-only; it is not a North America count.** `p572` adds Canada at grade `C†` below | Medium — survey + joint analysis |
| APAC (higher ed) | Not measured by use rate | **7 of 39** Australian universities document operational governance structures | 🟢 **A — primary**, a regulator's own 100%-response exercise; 🔴 but Australia-only, so it is not an APAC count either | Medium — but now **primary**: a regulator's own 100%-response exercise plus a peer-reviewed document analysis |


🟢 **`569-E` is closed here.** The ask was that the regional counts *state their
grade per count*, not merely a blended "source quality". They now do, on the
scale this KB has been using informally since `p569`:

| grade | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 🟢 **A** | primary instrument, named, with a stated denominator |
| 🔵 **B** | national survey or two agreeing independent surveys, but **not** region-wide |
| 🟡 **C** | single secondary source relaying a primary this session could not read |
| **†** | region assigned by inference (holder, language, ministry) rather than stated by the source |

🔴 **And writing the grades down exposes something the blended column was hiding:
three of the four rows are not actually regional counts.** `60%` is US, not North
America. `7 of 39` is Australia, not APAC. The EMEA governance figure blends
Europe *with* North America. 🔵 **Only the LATAM row is a genuine multi-country
regional measurement** — which inverts this table's apparent balance and should be
said out loud before any of it reaches a client deck.

🟢 **Re-measured first-hand this pass:** `vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp` (**MIT**, `310★`,
pushed `2026-11-01`, University of Illinois, `102` Canvas tools, FERPA-aware — 🟢 **licence
settled this pass; the ledger had it `unstated` across six pages**),
`jupyter/nbgrader` (**BSD-3-Clause**, `1,370★` — 🟢 also settled this pass),
`Submitty/Submitty` (**BSD-3-Clause**, `807★`, Rensselaer, with an explicit TA-grading
checkpoint), `openedx/codejail` and `openedx/XBlock` (**Apache-2.0**, `477★` / `471★`).

🟢 **The opportunity, sharpened by this pass's falsifier run:** the highest-starred
genuinely institution-accountable education artefact in this entire base is
`vardanagarwal/Proctoring-AI` at **`633★`, MIT** — against `134` AI bills tracked across
`31` states and `$169M` in US Department of Education FIPSE awards. 🔴 **The accountability
tier is where the money is and where the open supply runs out.** Build the defensibility
layer — audit trail, human-final-decision architecture, state-bill conformance — on
permissive North American components (`XBlock` + `codejail` + `nbgrader` + `canvas-mcp`)
and charge for the accountability, not the generator.

🔴 **One row to block:** `OS4ED/openSIS-Classic` (`347★`, pushed `2026-10-26`) is an
actively maintained student information system with **no licence file at all**. Current is
not the same as usable.


**p579 — 2026-11-01.**

🟢 **The learner-facing analytics seam on Canvas is open and under-built.**
`tl-its-umich-edu/my-learning-analytics` (Apache-2.0, `37★`, University of Michigan ITS) is
a *student*-facing analytics product — Resources Accessed, Assignment Planning, Grade
Distribution — not an administrator dashboard, and it integrates with **Canvas**. Set
against the `canvas-mcp` field (`280` repositories, head a `310★` MIT University of
Illinois server naming FERPA five times, `p565`), the two halves of a Canvas engagement are
both permissive and both institutionally authored. **Opportunity:** a FERPA-scoped,
student-facing analytics and advising layer on Canvas, assembled from Apache/MIT
institutional code rather than licensed from Instructure. 🔵 The regulatory frame is
settled enough to quote: `134` AI-in-education bills across `31` states in 2026, four
states (MD, ID, OK, VA) requiring both state guidance **and** mandatory district policy —
which makes *policy-conformance tooling* a sellable deliverable in its own right.

**p578 — 2026-10-31.** 🔴 No movement. The leg re-served the retired **"134 bills / 31
states (as of March 2026)"** figure for the third consecutive pass — treat the number above
as **provenance-flagged, not current**. 🟢 **What changed is the supply, not the demand:**
the largest dedicated tutoring agent this base has found outside APAC,
`plastic-labs/tutor-gpt` (**933★**), is **GPL-3.0**. **Opportunity:** this is the region
whose buyer most often arrives having already found that repository. The sellable answer is
the permissive stack — `Clew` (MIT) for sequencing, `Coding-Tutor`/DICT (Apache-2.0) for
evidence that the tutor works, `terracotta` (Apache-2.0) for randomised effect — attached to
Canvas over `ltijs`, with the licence argument made **once, in writing, in the proposal**.

**p577 — 2026-10-31.** Regulation is **per-state, not federal**: 134 bills across
31 states tracked as of March 2026 (privacy, classroom restrictions, curriculum),
plus COPPA constraints on anything K-12, plus a separate Canadian provincial
layer. Scope compliance per jurisdiction, never once for "the US".
Funding signal: US Dept of Education released $169M in FIPSE awards including
responsible-AI use (secondary sources; not confirmed against a primary source).
Market share estimates for the region range **36%–42%** across firms — direction
only. Vendor-survey adoption claims (e.g. "86% of institutions use generative AI")
are low confidence.
**Opportunity:** Canvas (AGPL-3.0) dominates higher ed, so the LTI-tool pattern
(`ltijs` + `DeepTutor`) plus **`terracotta` (Apache-2.0) for randomised
evidence of effect** is the differentiated offer — this buyer increasingly asks
for proof, not a demo. See `compose/patterns.md` P3.


#### 🆕 `p576` — the sanctioned-tooling gap, and the figures to quote as a range

🔴 **Quote NA as a range, never a point.** This pass's leg returned **`36 %` of the global
market valued at `USD 3.68 bn` for 2026** from one vendor and **`38 %` share for 2025** from
another, against a third claiming **`86 %` of educational organizations have adopted
generative AI** in the region. 🔵 Those are not reconcilable, and `p575` already recorded
that NA figures spread ~`2×` on 2026 and imply a `15.9 %` CAGR against this page's ~`41 %`
global.

🟢 **The durable, non-vendor facts:** around **`70 %` of institutions in Europe and North
America have or are developing AI guidance**; **federal money is moving** (reported
**`USD 169 m`** in FIPSE awards including responsible-AI use, Q1 2026 — 🔴 not confirmed in a
primary, because `federalregister.gov` and `ada.gov` remain unreachable, now for a fifth
pass, see `576-B`); and adoption in public schools is **budget-constrained**, not
interest-constrained.

🔵 **The shape of the NA engagement is unchanged and now twice-corroborated: usage is
already universal and invisible, and the institution's problem is sanctioning it** —
Gallup's **>`60 %` of college students using AI weekly against ~half of institutions
discouraging or prohibiting it** (`p575`). Add **COPPA** for anything touching under-13s,
and the **ADA Title II** dates (**`April 26, 2027`** / **`April 26, 2028`**) as the only
education obligation on these shelves with calendar dates.

#### 🔴 `p574` — the one dated education obligation in this region MOVED, and this base had never recorded it at all

🔴 **`Title II` appears `0` times in `573` passes of this base — README, gap
register and every shelf page.** It is the ADA's title covering state and local
government, and the DOJ web/mobile accessibility rule under it is the only
education compliance obligation on these shelves with **calendar dates**.

🔵 **What the rule requires:** conformance to **WCAG 2.1 Level AA** for web
content and mobile apps, explicitly including PDFs, Word documents, spreadsheets
and presentations posted by the entity. Public schools, districts and public
colleges and universities are covered as **instrumentalities** of state and local
government.

🔴 **What changed in 2026, and why a stale read is dangerous in both
directions:** an **interim final rule** extended both compliance dates by **one
year**.

| entity class | superseded date | current date |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Public entities, total population **≥ 50,000** | `April 24, 2026` | 🔴 **`April 26, 2027`** |
| Public entities **< 50,000**, and **special district** governments | `April 26, 2027` | 🔴 **`April 26, 2028`** |

🔵 The interim final rule is reported as published and effective **`April 20,
2026`**, with comments open to **`June 22, 2026`**. 🟢 **The technical standard did
not change and the obligation was not suspended** — only the dates moved.

🔴 **Grade `C†`, and the reason is a channel denial, not a research choice.** Both
primaries were attempted and neither resolved on this harness:

| primary attempted | result |
| --- | --- |
| 🔴 `WebFetch` → `www.ada.gov` (the rule's own page) | 🔴 **`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`** — outside this session's permitted network domains |
| 🔴 `WebFetch` → `www.federalregister.gov` (the rule text) | 🔴 **`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`** — same class |

🔵 **Ask (`574-E`): allow `ada.gov` and `federalregister.gov` in the session's
network settings.** Until then every date on this block is secondary. 🔴 **Two
secondary conflicts are left open rather than papered over:** one source dates the
superseded large-entity deadline `April 22, 2026` rather than `April 24`, and one
places **special districts at 2027 regardless of population** while the others say
2028. 🟡 A separate **HHS** Section 504 deadline in **May 2026** is reported as
still standing; single secondary, **do not quote**.

🟢 **What this does to the North America sale, and it sharpens `p572`'s finding
rather than replacing it.** `p572` established that the operative unit here is the
**school board**, not the ministry. This block gives that unit a **dated
obligation and a budget line**:

- 🟢 The only off-the-shelf remediation tool in the open-source supply is
  **North America-origin**: [`ucfopen/UDOIT`](https://github.com/ucfopen/UDOIT)
  (University of Central Florida), **GPL-3.0-only**, agreed across its licence
  file, `package.json` and `composer.json`. 🔴 Copyleft, so the engagement shape is
  **deploy-and-patch-upstream**, not vendor-into-product.
- 🟢 The agent layer that can run in CI over course content is **MIT**:
  [`Community-Access/accessibility-agents`](https://github.com/Community-Access/accessibility-agents)
  (`431★`, WCAG 2.2 AA) and
  [`plugin87/ux-ui-agent-skills`](https://github.com/plugin87/ux-ui-agent-skills)
  (`3.1k★`).
- 🔵 **The deliverable is the evidence trail, not the scan.** A date can move
  again; a dated per-course conformance inventory is what survives both the move
  and the complaint. Recipe: [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md)
  `P-576-A`.

🟡 **Demand-side signal, single secondary, grade `C`:** a Gallup study reported
via a newsletter puts **over `60 %` of college students using AI weekly** for
coursework while **roughly half** of their institutions discourage or prohibit it
— the same adoption-ahead-of-governance shape this page records for every region.
🔵 Market share claims for the region also disagree: `36 %` of the global market
at `$3.68B` (2026) → `$32B` (2030) from one vendor, `38 %` for 2025 from another,
and "dominant, `41.7 %` of forecast growth" from a third.


#### 🟡 `p572` — Canada, after five consecutive passes of a US-only North America leg

🟢 **The US-only gap is broken, by the same method that worked for LATAM: a
targeted query instead of a sixth re-run of the regional leg.** 🔴 At grade `C†` —
`www.otffeo.on.ca` also fails to resolve from this session, so the primary
discussion paper was **not** read.

🔵 **The finding is structural and it is useful precisely because it is negative:
Canada has no federal AI-in-education instrument, and the province is the wrong
unit too — the real unit is the school board.**

| level | state | grade |
| --- | --- | --: |
| Federal | No national AI-in-education rule; teacher federations describe the landscape as incoherent | 🟡 C |
| Provincial | **4 of 13** provinces/territories have issued formal AI guidance — **Ontario, Quebec, Alberta, British Columbia** | 🟡 C† |
| Ontario ministry | **No ministry memorandum on AI**; guidance comes from a provincial working group and from ECNO | 🟡 C† |
| School boards | The operative layer: **Ottawa Catholic** limits generative AI to ages **13+**; **Halton DSB** requires **parental consent**; **Peel DSB** publishes a teacher guide; **Upper Canada DSB** encourages supervised exploration | 🟡 C† |
| Board-level coverage | **42.3%** of Ontario boards reported to have any formal student AI policy (Fraser Institute, June 2026) | 🔴 **D — unverified against the Fraser Institute itself; do not quote** |
| Professional bodies | **Ontario Teachers' Federation** discussion paper (Jan/Mar 2026) asks for a **province-led AI in Education Advisory Council**; Quebec's *Prêt pour l'IA* recommends AI literacy from kindergarten to university | 🟡 C† |

🟢 **The engagement consequence, and it is a different sale from the US one.** In
the US this KB sells against a state-law patchwork. **In Canada the buyer is the
board**, there are ~`72` boards in Ontario alone, and **a majority appear to have
no policy at all** — so the sellable asset is a *policy-and-implementation
template* that a board can adopt, not a platform. 🔵 It also means **age-gating
and parental-consent handling are product requirements, not configuration**:
Ottawa Catholic's 13+ rule and Halton's consent requirement are already live
constraints that any deployed student-facing agent must satisfy on day one.

🔴 **`572-E` registered: three of the five Canadian claims above rest on a single
secondary source each, and the two primaries that would settle them
(`www.otffeo.on.ca`, and the Fraser Institute) are unreachable from this
session.** Canada is now *present* on this page rather than absent — at `C†`, and
labelled as such.

🟢 **New in the eighth pass (`p570`): two university-published assessment assets
placed from their grants** — [`ucbds-infra/ottr`](https://github.com/ucbds-infra/ottr)
(**UC Berkeley**, `cpyles@berkeley.edu`; an R autograding extension for
Otter-Grader, **BSD-3**) and
[`shmercer/writeAlizer`](https://github.com/shmercer/writeAlizer)
(**University of British Columbia**, `sterett.mercer@ubc.ca`; predicted
writing-quality scores). 🔵 **Note the second one is Canadian** — this base's
`North America` bucket is not a synonym for the United States, and a Canadian
engagement can be shown a Canadian-published asset. 🟢 **`ottr` is the
autograding half** of the assessment layer whose *gating* half (`itembank`) and
*calibration* half (`calibrate`) this base closed in the two prior passes.

**The regulatory surface is state-level and it is now large enough to be a
product.** A 2026 legislative tracker counts **134 AI-in-education bills across
31 states**, concentrated on data privacy, classroom restrictions and curriculum
integration. Four states — **Maryland, Idaho, Oklahoma and Virginia** — passed
2026 laws requiring *both* state guidance *and* mandatory district-level AI
policy adoption; Maryland's package (**SB 720**) also mandates AI coordinators.
**North Carolina's Session Law 2026-41 is the one with dates on it, and the first
is two months away:** a **state model policy due 31 December 2026**, **local
district policies due 30 June 2027**, and **teacher AI-literacy training due
30 June 2028**. That is a three-stage, statutory, date-certain pipeline in a
single state — the cleanest procurement trigger in the region and the one to lead
with, because the deadline sequence writes the engagement plan by itself. New York's
**A 9190** would restrict classroom AI to ninth grade and above, with exceptions
for diagnostics and special education. Federally, the **K-12 AI Literacy and
Readiness Act of 2026** has advanced out of House committee.

Governance lags use: **60% of US teachers** use AI tools (Gallup), while most
public schools have no formal student AI policy. A student-led coalition from all
50 states produced the **STUDENTS FIRST Act of 2026** framework with AASA in
August 2026, explicitly rejecting both an outright ban and unrestricted
adoption. Employer-side money is large: Microsoft, AWS and Caterpillar
commitments total roughly **USD 3.6 bn** in AI workforce and deployment spending.

**Opportunities.**
1. **District AI policy compliance-as-a-service.** Four states now *require*
   district-level policy and one requires named coordinators. This is a
   repeatable, deadline-driven engagement with a defined artefact. Nothing
   open-source addresses it.
2. **Canvas-attached agents.** Canvas dominates North American higher ed and has
   the strongest verified agent ecosystem anywhere — `vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp`
   (MIT, 310★) plus its agent skills. Highest-confidence technical starting point
   in the region.
3. **Accessibility remediation.** An **April 2026** deadline obliged large public
   institutions to meet updated ADA web-accessibility guidance. `canvas-mcp`
   already ships an accessibility-auditor skill — a narrow, verifiable,
   regulation-driven offer.
4. **Institutional repository RAG.** **DSpace** is **BSD-3-Clause** (see
   [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)) — the one place in the
   education stack where a client can own a proprietary derivative outright, over
   the corpus (theses, datasets, faculty output) a research agent most wants.

**Added on the fifth pass — three items, one of which changes a technical
recommendation above.**

- **California AB 1159 would bar using student data to train AI models.** This is
  a different kind of rule from the policy-and-guidance laws above: it constrains
  the *training* pipeline, not classroom use. For any engagement that touches
  Californian student records, it makes the **local-first** architecture a
  compliance feature rather than a preference — see
  [`OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA`](https://github.com/OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA) and
  `genspark-ai/genoffice` in [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).
  Status is a bill, not a law; do not quote it as enacted.
- **At least 29 states have a current or former AI-in-education task force or
  commission** (Education Commission of the States). That is the pre-legislative
  pipeline behind the 134 bills, and it is a mapped list of named bodies — i.e. a
  business-development target list, not just a statistic.
- **55% of high-school principals report their schools have not blocked or
  restricted AI tool access.** Read alongside "most schools have no formal
  student AI policy", this says the common state is **neither governed nor
  blocked** — unmanaged use. That is the honest framing for a district
  conversation, and it is a stronger opener than a compliance threat because it
  describes what the principal already knows.
- **Opportunity 3 above now has an open-source component, which it did not when
  it was written.**
  [`opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf`](https://github.com/opendataloader-project/opendataloader-pdf)
  (**Apache-2.0, 30,129★**) automates **PDF accessibility** and tagged-PDF/PDF-UA
  output. ADA-driven remediation of institutional document estates was previously
  scoped as audit-only via `canvas-mcp`'s auditor skill; it can now be scoped as
  **audit plus automated remediation**, which is a materially larger engagement.
  See pattern `P-570-B`.

#### Canada (inside North America — not a separate region value)

Education is **exclusively provincial**, so there is no national policy to find —
a structural fact, not a research gap. Quebec has a 2024 guide and a **March 2025
directive suspending AI assistants**; Ontario has no ministry memorandum and only
**42.3%** of its boards have a student AI policy. The opportunity is
province-by-province policy and procurement work, and it does not generalise
across the border.


**Sixth pass (`p568`) — the state-mandate wave now has a delivery component, and
an assessment component that answers its data politics.**

- **The mandates are curriculum, and curriculum is a production problem.** The
  2026 state wave is AI *literacy* instruction with dates attached — Illinois
  requires it for grades 6–12 **effective 2026-08-01**; Idaho's SB 1227 embeds it
  in general education for incoming undergraduates from **Fall 2026** and requires
  institutions to evaluate AI tools for bias; Florida's State Board approved rules
  covering K-12 **and its 28 state colleges**; Maryland's SB 720 requires
  statewide K-12 guidelines. 🟢 The deliverable those buy is **narrated, captioned
  courseware at volume**, and the sixth pass placed the engine for it:
  [`jhudsl/ari`](https://github.com/jhudsl/ari) (**MIT**, Johns Hopkins Data
  Science Lab) with [`galaxyproject/video-lectures`](https://github.com/galaxyproject/video-lectures)
  (**MIT**) as the reference pipeline. 🔴 Swap its Amazon Polly stage for
  `abogen` (**MIT**) if the district's procurement asks where the audio is
  generated — and `abogen` emits **synchronised captions**, which is an
  accessibility line item in US K-12 procurement.
- **Student-data politics is the real gate, and `itembank` is built for it.**
  California's proposed **A.B. 1159** would bar training models on student data
  unless it directly benefits the school; Idaho's law adds bias evaluation and
  privacy duties. 🟢 [`onionviolet/itembank`](https://github.com/onionviolet/itembank)
  (**MIT**) is **local-first with nothing leaving the machine but an optional
  update check**, and **no model sits in its scoring path** — short-answer items
  are never auto-graded. That is a procurement answer, not a feature.
- 🔵 **Federal position unchanged and still non-binding:** the April 2025 executive
  order sets priorities, and a **K-12 AI Literacy and Readiness Act** has been
  advanced in committee — a possible funding pathway, not law. Guidance still
  heavily outnumbers enacted statute, and the picture is state-by-state. Canada
  remains provincial (see above); Mexico is covered under LATAM.

🟢 **[`finite-sample/guess`](https://github.com/finite-sample/guess) (MIT) is
placed North America, and the placement channel is new.** Its copyright line
names only two individuals — which under this base's own instrument places
nothing — but `master/DESCRIPTION`'s `Authors@R` field carries
**`mcor@ualberta.ca`**: the **University of Alberta**. 🔵 **For the R tier, a
CRAN manifest places a package where the grant cannot.** Registered `569-D`.

🔵 **Regional read, this pass (secondary sources, treated as directional).**
North America remains the largest regional market on every source that splits it
out, at **36–38% share** (one vendor puts 2026 at **USD 3.68 bn**); the
**USD 169 m** in FIPSE awards including responsible-AI use is the most concrete
public funding signal. 🔴 **Governance is still state-by-state, not federal** —
**134 bills across 31 states** tracked as of March 2026 — and a multi-country
engagement must reconcile Canadian federal/provincial requirements with US state
law. COPPA remains the binding constraint in the child-facing segment.

🟢 **The opportunity this pass creates here is assurance work, not discovery.**
`P-572-A` exists because certification and awarding bodies under accreditation
review must defend how item parameters were estimated and how forms were
equated. The permissive stack to do that is now complete (see
[`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md)) and **every component of it
is pre-adoption software** — which makes the cross-validation harness the
billable deliverable rather than a caveat.

🔴 **Declared gap, stated plainly: the `AI education North America 2026 adoption
regulation players` leg returned ZERO repository-level assets.** It returned

pass in which the regional search legs produced no new address in any region —
